**BYD Auto Co., Ltd.** ([Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simplified_Chinese_characters): 比亚迪汽车; [pinyin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pinyin): *Bǐyàdí Qìchē*) is the [automotive](163778.md) subsidiary of [BYD Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Company), a [publicly listed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Publicly_listed) Chinese multinational [manufacturing company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manufacturing_company). It manufactures passenger [battery electric vehicles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battery_electric_vehicle) (BEVs) and [plug-in hybrid electric vehicles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plug-in_hybrid_electric_vehicles) (PHEVs)—collectively known as [new energy vehicles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plug-in_electric_vehicles_in_China) (NEVs) in China—along with [electric buses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electric_bus) and [electric trucks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electric_truck). The company sells its vehicles under its main BYD brand as well as its high-end brands, which are [Denza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denza), [Fangchengbao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fangchengbao) and [Yangwang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yangwang), and its commercial-focused brand, [Linghui](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Linghui).[^cite_note-11]

BYD Auto was established in January 2003 as a subsidiary of BYD Company, a battery manufacturer, following the acquisition and restructuring of Xi'an Qinchuan Automobile. The first car designed by BYD, the petrol engined [BYD F3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_F3), began production in 2005. In 2008, BYD launched its first plug-in hybrid electric vehicle, the [BYD F3DM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_F3DM), followed by the [BYD e6](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_e6), its first battery electric vehicle, in 2009.

Since 2020, BYD Auto has experienced substantial sales growth that is driven by the increasing market share of new energy vehicles in China. The company has expanded into overseas markets from 2021, mainly to Europe, Southeast Asia, Oceania and the Americas. In 2022, BYD ended production of purely [internal combustion engined](41228673.md) vehicles to focus on new energy vehicles.

The company is characterised by its extensive [vertical integration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vertical_integration), leveraging BYD group's expertise in producing batteries and other related components such as [electric motors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electric_motor) and [electronic controls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electronic_control_unit). Most components used in BYD vehicles are claimed to be produced in-house within the group. As of 2024, BYD's [battery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electric_battery) subsidiary [FinDreams Battery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FinDreams_Battery) is the world's second largest producer of [electric vehicle batteries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electric_vehicle_batteries) behind [CATL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CATL).[^cite_note-12] It specialises in [lithium iron phosphate (LFP) batteries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithium_iron_phosphate_battery), including BYD's proprietary [Blade battery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Blade_battery).

BYD has become the best-selling car brand in China in 2023, after surpassing [Volkswagen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volkswagen), which had held the title since the [liberalisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_liberalization) of the [Chinese automotive industry](8518766.md).[^cite_note-BloombergVW-13] In 2025, nearly 77 percent of BYD's sales came from the Chinese market.[^cite_note-14] BYD is also the third most valuable car manufacturer in the world, based on [market capitalization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Market_capitalization).[^cite_note-15] In 2025, BYD became the world's largest electric carmaker, overtaking [Tesla](5533631.md),[^cite_note-16] and overtook [Ford](30433662.md) to be 6th in total automobile sales.[^cite_note-17]

The company has faced scrutiny and criticism related to its business practices, including allegations of aggressive price reductions, labor issues at its facilities, and various environmental concerns.[^cite_note-\:22-18][^cite_note-\:14-19][^cite_note-\:15-20]

## History

### 2003–2008: Establishment and early years

BYD Company was founded in 1995 as a battery manufacturer. In January 2003, BYD Company founder [Wang Chuanfu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wang_Chuanfu) established BYD Auto after acquiring and renaming a struggling small automotive manufacturer, Xi'an Qinchuan Automobile, from the [state-owned](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State-owned) [defence](608296.md) company [Norinco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norinco). The company was acquired for [HK\$](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hong_Kong_dollar)269 million in exchange for a 77% stake, shortly after BYD raised HK\$1.6 billion on the [Hong Kong Stock Exchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hong_Kong_Stock_Exchange) in July 2002.[^cite_note-Sina_Finance-2023-21][^cite_note-22][^cite_note-23][^cite_note-24] The acquisition was met with disapproval from shareholders, as the plan had not been disclosed in the [prospectus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prospectus_\(finance\)).[^cite_note-Breevoort-2021-25] Wang Chuanfu acquired Qinchuan with the intention of developing battery-powered electric vehicles, leveraging BYD's expertise in battery manufacturing.[^cite_note-26][^cite_note-27] As Qinchuan had been manufacturing cars since 1987, the purchase gave BYD access to car manufacturing technology and an automobile production licence that was difficult to obtain at that time.[^cite_note-Schenau-28][^cite_note-29] At the time of the acquisition, Qinchuan was producing a small car called the QCJ7181 Flyer, which BYD renamed the [BYD Flyer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Flyer) from 2005. Limited capacity at the former Qinchuan manufacturing plant in [Xi'an](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xi'an), [Shaanxi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shaanxi) prompted BYD to construct a new manufacturing plant in the Xi'an Development Zone within the city.[^cite_note-Quartz-2018-30]

The first car developed by BYD, codenamed 316, was rejected by dealers due to its poor styling and was scrapped before reaching the market, writing off [CN¥](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CN%C2%A5)100 million in [research and development](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Research_and_development) (R\&D) expenses.[^cite_note-Mehta-2021-31] Wang Chuanfu personally destroyed the [prototype](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prototype).[^cite_note-32] The company instead developed the [BYD F3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_F3) sedan, which entered production on 16 April 2005, with a price of CN¥73,000 (approximately US\$10,000). Bearing a resemblance to the [Toyota Corolla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toyota_Corolla) but offered at a lower price, the F3 gained popularity and became a successful model, with over 63,000 units sold in its first year.[^cite_note-Quartz-2018-30][^cite_note-Mehta-2021-31] The 100,000th F3 rolled off the assembly line on 18 June 2007, 20 months after production began. Following this success, the larger [BYD F6](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_F6) sedan, modelled after the [Honda Accord](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Honda_Accord), entered production in August 2007.[^cite_note-33][^cite_note-34] Initially, BYD cars were equipped with older [Mitsubishi Motors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mitsubishi_Motors) engines manufactured in China, but within a few years BYD Auto developed its own engines by modifying Mitsubishi engine designs.[^cite_note-Breevoort-2021-25][^cite_note-35][^cite_note-1miModel3-36][^cite_note-37]



The [BYD F3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_F3), the first vehicle designed by BYD, was produced from 2005 to 2019.

During its early years, BYD Auto achieved growth by [reverse engineering](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reverse_engineering) competitors' products and supplier parts, and by maintaining tight control over costs. The BYD F3, the company's first mass-produced passenger car, has been described as "a copycat" of the [Toyota Corolla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toyota_Corolla).[^cite_note-38] The [BYD F0](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_F0) was described as "a clear copy" of the [Toyota Aygo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toyota_Aygo),[^cite_note-39] and the [BYD S8](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_S8) bears a similar appearance to the [Mercedes-Benz SL-Class](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercedes-Benz_SL-Class_\(R230\)), with an "almost identical" [Mercedes-Benz CLK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercedes-Benz_CLK-Class_\(C209\)) front end and a [Renault Mégane CC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renault_M%C3%A9gane#M%C3%A9gane_III_\(2008%E2%80%932016\)) rear.[^cite_note-40][^cite_note-41] Wang Chuanfu responded by stating the company used only "non-[patented](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patent) technologies".[^cite_note-42] In 2009, the United States government was advised by its [consulate general in Guangzhou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Consulate_General_of_the_United_States,_Guangzhou) that BYD employed an approach of "copying and then modifying car designs." However, according to the consul, BYD had modified the vehicle designs sufficiently to satisfy Chinese courts that no patents had been infringed.[^cite_note-\:7-43]



The BYD stand at the 2009 Central China High-Tech Fair in [Shenzhen](83368.md)

In 2005, BYD Auto contributed 10% of BYD Company's total revenue. As its automotive business expanded, this share rose to 25% by 2006. In the first half of 2009, BYD Auto accounted for 55% of the parent company's total revenue, the first time its automotive division had contributed more than half of overall earnings.[^cite_note-Sina_Finance-2023-21] By 2008, BYD Auto operated two vehicle assembly plants in [Xi'an](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xi'an) and [Shenzhen](83368.md) with a combined production capacity of 300,000 units per year, alongside an R\&D and testing centre in [Shanghai](27643.md) and a [moulding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Molding_\(process\)) plant in [Beijing](18603746.md).[^cite_note-1miModel3-36]

Following years of sales growth, widespread withdrawal of BYD [dealerships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Car_dealership) was reported in major Chinese cities during 2010, attributable to excessively rapid expansion, a limited model range, and [internal competition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internal_competition) that created difficulties for dealers. The situation was compounded by BYD's emphasis on production capacity over product quality. In response, BYD reduced its annual production target from 800,000 to 600,000 vehicles; however, actual output fell short of the revised target, reaching only 517,000 units that year. Over the subsequent three years, BYD redirected its focus toward resolving concerns related to quality, dealer channel management, and brand development.[^cite_note-Sina_Finance-2023-21]

### 2006–2020: New energy vehicle efforts and sales stagnation



Picture of BYD's first experimental electric vehicle, labeled the "BYD-001 (Test)"

BYD began exploring hybrid and electric vehicles as early as 1997, before acquiring Xi'an Qinchuan Automobile. In that year, Wang Chuanfu, as part of his research into electric vehicles, purchased a BJ6490D, an electric car made by [Beijing Second Auto Works](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BAW) that was built off an imported [knock-down kit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Knock-down_kit) of the [Holden Commodore VN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holden_Commodore_\(VN\)) station wagon. The BJ6490D was powered by [golf cart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golf_cart) batteries, with a range of about 100 km (62 mi) and a top speed of 92 km/h (57 mph). Later, BYD acquired a second BJ6490D, later converted into a hybrid vehicle.[^cite_note-44][^cite_note-45]

At the 2004 [Beijing Auto Show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing_Auto_Show), alongside its petrol-powered concept cars, BYD unveiled the BYD Flyer EF3, an electric sedan. Based on the BYD Flyer, the Flyer EF3 was initially intended for production in 2005, with a focus on serving as a taxi in Shenzhen. However, this plan did not materialise.[^cite_note-46] Two years later at the 2006 Beijing Auto Show, BYD showcased another electric car, the BYD F3e. Based on the BYD F3, it had an [all-electric range](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All-electric_range) of more than 300 km (186 mi), and was planned to be produced within three years from 2007. In December 2010, a BYD Auto general manager confirmed that the company had cancelled plans for its production due to the lack of support in charging infrastructure.[^cite_note-Beissmann-2010-47]

In 2006, BYD established the Electric Vehicle Research Institute, dedicated to the R\&D and trial production of parts for new energy vehicles and the whole vehicle, including battery electric vehicles and plug-in hybrid vehicles.[^cite_note-\:0-48]

In 2008, Wang Chuanfu set a goal for the company to become the leader in the Chinese automotive market by 2015, and the largest car manufacturer globally by 2025.[^cite_note-\:0-48] Wang stated that electric vehicles would serve as BYD's "stepping stone" to skip the development of internal combustion engine vehicles and facilitate the company's entry into international markets, including Europe and North America.[^cite_note-49]



The 2008 [BYD F3DM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_F3DM) at the BYD Museum. It is the first modern, mass-produced [plug-in hybrid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plug-in_hybrid) vehicle globally.

In March 2008, the [plug-in hybrid electric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plug-in_hybrid) version of the BYD F3, the [F3DM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_F3DM), was introduced as the world's first [production model](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Production_model) plug-in hybrid car at the [Geneva Motor Show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geneva_Motor_Show) in Switzerland.[^cite_note-50][^cite_note-Anderson-2012-51] Initially, the F3DM was offered in China as a [fleet vehicle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fleet_vehicle) for governments, banks and other institutions, until [retail sales](183515.md) started in March 2010 when the Chinese government started granting subsidies for new energy vehicles as part of the Government’s overall total R\&D investment of 230 billion.[^cite_note-52][^cite_note-53] The vehicle had low sales, with only 100 units sold by 2011.[^cite_note-\:7-43]

In January 2009, BYD introduced its first production battery electric vehicle, the e6 at the 2009 [North American International Auto Show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_American_International_Auto_Show) in [Detroit](8687.md).[^cite_note-54] It is powered by a battery pack codenamed "Fe", which provides a claimed electric range of 400 km (249 mi).[^cite_note-55] While plans to sell the vehicle to the general public in the US were shelved, the company sold the e6 in the US as a [fleet vehicle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fleet_vehicle) in a limited number.[^cite_note-56][^cite_note-57][^cite_note-feb2016-58]

In May 2009, [Volkswagen AG](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volkswagen_AG) considered forming a partnership with BYD in the area of hybrids and electric vehicles, having signed a [memorandum of understanding](1018279.md).[^cite_note-59] Volkswagen withdrew from the partnership after conducting [due diligence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Due_diligence).[^cite_note-\:7-43]



The [BYD e6](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_e6), showcased at the 2010 [North American International Auto Show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_American_International_Auto_Show) in [Detroit](8687.md), US

In July 2009, BYD acquired Hunan [Midea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midea_Group) Coach Company, a bus manufacturer based in [Hunan](166409.md), allowing it to manufacture buses and coaches and enter the commercial vehicle market. The plant in [Changsha, Hunan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Changsha,_Hunan), reportedly received a CN¥ 3 billion investment for development.[^cite_note-60] BYD started producing [battery electric buses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battery_electric_bus) as part of a pilot scheme initiated by the Chinese government.[^cite_note-O-2022-61] It signed a deal to supply 1,000 [BYD K9](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_K9) electric buses to the [Hunan](166409.md) Government in China. The buses have a range of 305 km (190 mi) per charge with a top speed of 70 km/h (43 mph), a charging time of six hours and 50% fast charging in 30 minutes.[^cite_note-Beissmann-2010-47]

In 2010, BYD Auto Industry Co., Ltd. and Daimler AG, now known as [Mercedes-Benz Group AG](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercedes-Benz_Group_AG), formed a 50-50 [joint venture](538782.md) named Shenzhen BYD Daimler New Technology with a brand named [Denza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denza) to focus on research and development of new energy vehicles. The brand showcased a concept car called the Denza EV at [Auto China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auto_China) in April 2012.[^cite_note-62] Denza was later restructured in 2021, when BYD took control of the brand by taking a 90% stake in the venture.[^cite_note-O-2022-61] Mercedes-Benz withdrew entirely from the joint venture in September 2024.[^cite_note-\:03-63]

In August 2013, BYD launched the Dynasty Series product line with the introduction of the [BYD Qin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Qin), a plug-in hybrid electric vehicle variant of the petrol-engine [BYD Surui](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Surui). The Qin was designed to replace the [BYD F3DM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_F3DM), an earlier plug-in hybrid model. The Qin quickly became the best-selling plug-in electric vehicle of early 2014.[^cite_note-64][^cite_note-65][^cite_note-66]

In November 2016, the company hired [Wolfgang Egger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wolfgang_Egger) as BYD Auto's head of design. Egger, who built his career at [Alfa Romeo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alfa_Romeo), [Audi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Audi), and [SEAT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SEAT), created his first design for BYD, the Dynasty concept, a concept electric SUV that was displayed at the 2017 [Shanghai Auto Show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shanghai_Auto_Show) and previewed the design of the [second-generation BYD Tang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Tang#Second_generation_\(2018\)).[^cite_note-67][^cite_note-68][^cite_note-69]

Before 2020, BYD heavily relied on [government subsidies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_subsidies) to generate profit from its plug-in hybrid and battery electric vehicles. In 2016, the company received approximately [US\$](18717338.md)1 billion in [new energy vehicle subsidies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_incentives_for_plug-in_electric_vehicles), surpassing its net profit for that year, and accounted for over 20% of BYD's US\$5 billion in revenue from new energy vehicle sales that year.[^cite_note-70] Rhodium Group estimates that BYD received approximately US\$4.3 billion in state support between 2015 and 2020.[^cite_note-71]

Between 2017 and 2019, BYD faced challenges due to a reduction in government subsidies.[^cite_note-72][^cite_note-73][^cite_note-74][^cite_note-75] This led to a significant slowdown in sales and a sharp decline in net profit over three consecutive years. In 2019, the company's net profit dropped to only CN¥1.6 billion. During this period, Wang Chuanfu stated that the company's primary goal was merely "to survive".[^cite_note-76]

### 2020–present: rapid growth and global expansion

BYD experienced a substantial surge in vehicle sales from 2020 to 2024. The company sold 4,272,145 vehicles globally in 2024, a ten-fold increase compared to the 2020 figure of 427,302 vehicles. The increase was partly attributed to the increasing popularity of new energy vehicles in China, which accounted for 27.5% of vehicle sales in China in 2022, up from 5.8% in 2020.[^cite_note-77][^cite_note-O-2023-78]



A [BYD Han EV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Han) in a charging station

&#x20;BYD introduced its first vehicle equipped with the [Blade battery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blade_battery), the [BYD Han](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Han) large sedan, in early 2020. The Han went on sale in July 2020 with an option of plug-in hybrid electric (Han DM) and battery electric (Han EV) variants.[^cite_note-Kane-2020-79] The range-topping variant of the Han EV was claimed to be the fastest electric car in China, while the DM version was the fastest hybrid sedan.[^cite_note-80] BYD announced its entry to the European passenger car market in May 2020, starting with Norway. The first batch of 100 [BYD Tang EVs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Tang) equipped with the Blade battery were sent to Norway in June 2021.[^cite_note-81]



The [BYD Atto 3 / Yuan Plus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Atto_3) helped increase the sales of BYDs globally; it is one of the best selling electric cars in Australia.

In April 2021, BYD introduced the e-Platform 3.0, a third-generation [platform](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Car_platform) for battery electric vehicles that integrated and standardised core components along with a new body structure, new electrical architecture, and [operating system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operating_system).[^cite_note-82] The platform began production starting with the [BYD Dolphin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Dolphin) and the [BYD Yuan Plus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Yuan_Plus) that were announced in August 2021.[^cite_note-Kane-2021-83][^cite_note-84] It also established the Ocean Series line of products, which consists of models named after [marine animals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marine_animals).[^cite_note-85] In December 2021, Daimler AG reduced its stake in its joint venture brand with BYD Auto from 50% to 10%, with BYD Auto controlling 90%. BYD refreshed Denza's line-up with the release of the [Denza D9](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denza_D9) minivan in 2022, followed by the [Denza N7](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denza_N7) SUV in 2023.[^cite_note-Zhang/CnEVPost-2021-86] In March 2022, BYD ended the production of pure [internal combustion engine](41228673.md) vehicles.[^cite_note-\:11-87]

In January 2023, BYD established its second premium brand called [Yangwang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yangwang) by introducing the [Yangwang U8](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yangwang_U8) plug-in hybrid electric large SUV,[^cite_note-88][^cite_note-89] and the [Yangwang U9](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yangwang_U9) battery electric [supercar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supercar).[^cite_note-Quick-2023-90] The company further expanded its brand portfolio by introducing the [Fangchengbao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fangchengbao) brand in June 2023, which focuses on [off-road vehicles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Off-road_vehicles).[^cite_note-91]



2023 BYD Seagull

In April 2023, BYD released its smallest and cheapest battery electric vehicle called the [Seagull](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Seagull). It features a 4-seater, 5-door configuration in a [hatchback](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hatchback) body style, while being priced in a segment dominated by smaller 3-door cars. The car became a sales success in China. On 29 November 2023, BYD produced the 200,000th Seagull after only seven months in the market.[^cite_note-92][^cite_note-93] Due to its low cost of production and its low selling price at below CN¥90,000 or US\$12,000, the Seagull has garnered curiosity and praise from international media and industry experts.[^cite_note-94][^cite_note-95][^cite_note-96][^cite_note-97]

While celebrating BYD's 5,000,000th new energy vehicle production in August 2023, Wang Chuanfu called on local Chinese car manufacturers to "unite" to take on foreign manufacturers, responding to the severe [price war in the Chinese market](8518766.md#Price_war_\(2022%E2%80%93present\)) throughout 2023. Wang claims that it is "an emotional need for the 1.4 billion Chinese people to see a Chinese brand becoming global," and initiated a campaign titled "Together, we are Chinese autos" ([Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simplified_Chinese_characters): 在一起，才是中国汽车). The call was welcomed by the CEOs of [Nio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nio_Inc.) and [Li Auto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Li_Auto).[^cite_note-98][^cite_note-99]

On 18 November 2024, BYD held an event in Shenzhen to celebrate its 30th anniversary and becoming the first automaker globally to produce 10,000,000 new energy vehicles. The milestone vehicle was a [Denza Z9](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denza_Z9).[^cite_note-100]

In early 2023 and early 2024, BYD introduced the Champion Edition and Honor Edition versions of its models, respectively, featuring price reductions aimed at expanding market share in the Chinese market. The strategy contributed to an [intensified price war in the market](8518766.md#Price_war_\(2022%E2%80%93present\)), prompting Chinese authorities to advise BYD to avoid further price reductions. In response, on 10 February 2025, BYD introduced Smart Driving Edition versions across 21 models, featuring enhanced [advanced driver assistance systems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Advanced_driver_assistance_systems) (ADAS) marketed as [TianShen](#TianShen), including city-level NOA (Navigate on Autopilot), with prices starting at 100,000 yuan. The update aims to offer additional features without increasing prices.[^cite_note-\:13-101]

In July 2025, BYD recorded its first monthly delivery drop of the year, shipping 341,030 vehicles compared with 377,628 in June, and nearly flat versus the same month in 2024.[^cite_note-102] In the second quarter of 2025, BYD posted its first profit decline in more than three years, with net income down 29.9% year-on-year to 6.4 billion yuan.[^cite_note-103] In September 2025, BYD announced internally and to its suppliers had cut its annual sales target by up to 16% to 4.6 million units, down from the 5.5 million originally guided in March.[^cite_note-104] Wang Chuanfu stated the reason for the sales drop was because BYD's technological lead were not as strong as the previous years, and promised to launch "major technological announcements" in 2026.[^cite_note-105][^cite_note-106] Despite the cut, by the end of 2025, BYD had overtaken [Ford Motor Company](30433662.md) in terms of vehicles sold per year.[^cite_note-107] The "major technological announcements" were revealed in March 2026, which included the second-generation Blade battery alongside an ultra-fast "Flash Charging" technology, enabling compatible vehicles to charge from a 10% to 70% state of charge in 5 minutes, and from 10% to 97% in 9 minutes.[^cite_note-\:19-108]

## Marketing

### Branding

See also: [BYD Company § Name](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Company#Name)

BYD Auto's original logo was used between 2003 and 2007. According to the company, the blue and white colours represent the sky and the clouds respectively.[^cite_note-109][^cite_note-110] The logo was criticised due to its resemblance to the [BMW roundel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BMW_roundel).[^cite_note-111][^cite_note-112] It was replaced with the logo used by its parent company upon the introduction of the [BYD F1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_F1) (renamed to F0 later).[^cite_note-113] On 1 January 2021, BYD Auto adopted a new brand logo, while other BYD businesses retained the older logo.[^cite_note-114] On 17 February 2022, the logo of BYD Auto was slightly revised with a narrower width to follow [graphic design](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Graphic_design) trends, coinciding with the introduction of the new BYD Company logo.[^cite_note-115]

As the name "BYD" had no particular meaning, BYD Auto started adopting the slogan "Build Your Dreams" since it participated in the 2008 [North American International Auto Show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_American_International_Auto_Show) in the US.[^cite_note-116] In 2017, when the company released the [second-generation BYD Tang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Tang#second), BYD Auto started placing "Build Your Dreams" badging at the rear of its vehicles, replacing the standard oval BYD logo.[^cite_note-117] The badging was retained until late 2023, when the company announced that the badging would be dropped in favour of the three-letter BYD logo due to widespread criticisms.[^cite_note-118][^cite_note-119]

#### Logo history

### Advertising and sponsorships



BYD stand at UEFA Euro 2024 Fan Zone [Hamburg](13467.md), Germany

In December 2016, BYD signed American actor [Leonardo DiCaprio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leonardo_DiCaprio) as the brand's global brand ambassador for new energy vehicles.[^cite_note-120]

In January 2024, BYD signed a deal with [UEFA](32332.md) to be the official partner of [UEFA European Football Championship 2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_Euro_2024), replacing Volkswagen as the competition's mobility partner.[^cite_note-121] Later, in May, BYD was announced by [CONMEBOL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CONMEBOL) as an official partner for the [2024 Copa América](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Copa_Am%C3%A9rica).[^cite_note-122] In October 2024, BYD announced a global strategic partnership with [Game Science](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Game_Science)'s action role-playing game *[Black Myth: Wukong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_Myth:_Wukong)*, becoming its exclusive automotive brand partner.[^cite_note-123]

## Global markets

| Rank | Location | Vehicle sales (2025) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | [China](5405.md) | 3,528,545[^cite_note-124] |
| 2 | [Brazil](3383.md) | 112,929[^cite_note-125] |
| 3 | [Mexico](3966054.md) | 75,157[^cite_note-126] |
| 4 | [Indonesia](14579.md) | 54,185[^cite_note-127] |
| 5 | [Australia](4689264.md) | 52,415[^cite_note-128] |
| 6 | [United Kingdom](31717.md) | 51,422[^cite_note-129] |
| 7 | [Turkey](11125639.md) | 45,537[^cite_note-130] |
| 8 | [Thailand](30128.md) | 44,666[^cite_note-131] |

BYD started exporting its cars in 2005 to Russia, when the company introduced the BYD Flyer hatchback at the 2005 Moscow International Motor Show.[^cite_note-132] In 2009, BYD started exporting cars to Africa, South America, and the Middle East.[^cite_note-133] Since 2021, BYD started expanding its global presence rapidly by prioritizing exports of passenger electric vehicles. Beginning in 2022, BYD has committed to producing right-hand drive versions of several battery electric passenger models for exports to [left-hand traffic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Left-hand_traffic) countries such as Australia, the UK and Thailand.[^cite_note-134] These include newer models with BYD's third-generation platform (e-Platform 3.0) such as the [Atto 3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Atto_3) (the export version of the [Yuan Plus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Yuan_Plus)), [Dolphin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Dolphin) and [Seal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Seal).[^cite_note-135] For the Dolphin, the company had also done an extensive reengineering to ensure the small car would achieve the highest ratings in [Euro NCAP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euro_NCAP) and [Australasian NCAP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australasian_New_Car_Assessment_Program) testing.[^cite_note-136][^cite_note-137]

Throughout 2023, the company exported over 242,766 passenger new energy vehicles, a year-over-year increase of 334 percent.[^cite_note-CNC2023-4] As of 2023, BYD Auto vehicles are sold in over 70 countries.[^cite_note-138] In 2024, BYD sold 417,204 vehicles overseas.[^cite_note-\:10-139] According to [Stella Li](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stella_Li), the executive vice president of BYD, the company is aiming to deliver 50% of its production output outside its home market.[^cite_note-140] However, an automotive analyst from [Bloomberg Intelligence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bloomberg_Intelligence) China believes that the target is unlikely to be achieved until 2030 or later.[^cite_note-141]

### Africa

#### South Africa



BYD Tygervalley dealership, [Cape Town](6653.md)

BYD entered the [South African](17416221.md) market in 2023, when it hosted an event to launch its [Atto 3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Atto_3) EV. The company noted stronger demand for greener alternatives in the [South African automotive market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Automotive_industry_in_South_Africa), and said that due to the size of SA's economy, there was significant opportunity for electric vehicles such as its own.[^cite_note-\:17-142] At launch, BYD also noted the South African government's commitment to significantly reduce greenhouse gas emissions by 2030, stating that this aligned with the company's goal of promoting sustainable transportation by reducing carbon footprints through its technology.[^cite_note-\:17-142] BYD has since launched many other models in South Africa, and in 2025, had 18 dealerships across the country, with a plan to increase its network to 30 by the end of Q1 2026.[^cite_note-\:18-143] The company also builds [electric buses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electric_bus) for major [Cape Town](6653.md) bus operator [Golden Arrow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_Arrow_Bus_Services). The latter has around 100 BYD eBus B12s as part of its fleet, with plans to swap all of its diesel buses to electric ones over time.[^cite_note-144] As of December 2025, BYD sells its Sealion 7, Shark 6, Seal, Atto 3, Sealion 6, Sealion 5, Dolphin, and Dolphin Surf models in SA.[^cite_note-145] BYD is also the largest player in the South African public EV charger sector, and is currently rolling out up to 300 [charging stations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charging_station) across the country.[^cite_note-\:18-143]

### Asia

#### Japan



BYD K9 bus in [Morioka, Iwate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morioka,_Iwate), Japan

In 2015, BYD became the first Chinese manufacturer in Japan to market electric buses. It supplied the [K9](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_K9) large electric bus to bus operator [Princess Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Princess_Line_\(bus_operator\)?action=edit\&redlink=1) in [Kyoto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyoto).[^cite_note-146][^cite_note-147] In 2022, BYD held a 70% market share of electric buses in Japan.[^cite_note-148]

In July 2022, BYD announced that sales of its passenger electric cars in Japan would start in 2023.[^cite_note-149] Sales of the BYD Atto 3 began in February 2023, with the company's first sales outlet located in [Yokohama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yokohama). BYD sells its vehicles in Japan through a [dealership](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Car_dealership) network rather than through direct sales.[^cite_note-150] The BYD Dolphin was added to the local line-up in September 2023. All BYD models sold in Japan are adapted to local conditions by adopting the [CHAdeMO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CHAdeMO) charging standard.[^cite_note-151]

At the [2025 Japan Mobility Show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tokyo_Motor_Show) held in October, BYD unveiled the [BYD Racco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Racco), a battery electric [kei car](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kei_car) designed exclusively for the Japanese market.[^cite_note-152] The Racco is the first purpose-built kei car model from a non-Japanese manufacturer.[^cite_note-153] The Racco will be manufactured in China and exported to Japan, with sales starting in summer 2026.[^cite_note-154]

In December 2025, BYD launched the [Sealion 6](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Sealion_6) in Japan, marking the company's first [plug-in hybrid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plug-in_hybrid_electric_vehicle) offering in the country. It was the lowest-priced plug-in hybrid SUV available in Japan at the time of launch.[^cite_note-Leung_2025-155] BYD noted that the Sealion 6 had been adapted for the Japanese market, with enhanced rear-seat comfort and reduced vibration based on local consumer feedback.[^cite_note-Leung_2025-155]

#### South Korea

In 2016, BYD signed a deal to have 3 of its vehicles, K9 electric buses, operated in South Korea for the first time.[^cite_note-156] It launched as the eBUS-12 in 2017, in a ceremony attended by [Ministry of Environment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Climate,_Energy_and_Environment) officials.[^cite_note-157][^cite_note-158] In April 2018, the eBus-7 model began running on [Jeju Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeju_Island). In June, the Ministry of Environment began to subsidize the eBUS-12. The bus was also used for the [Daejeon](284812.md) government's pilot intra-city electric bus service that year.[^cite_note-159] In 2023, BYD opened an office in [Seoul](19159283.md).[^cite_note-160] In January 2025, BYD began selling EVs in the South Korean consumer market.[^cite_note-161] In December 2025, BYD overtook [General Motors Korea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Motors_Korea) in terms of unit sales in the South Korean market.[^cite_note-162]

#### Hong Kong

BYD started sales of its passenger electric cars in Hong Kong in September 2022, shortly after it finished developing the right-hand drive Atto 3.[^cite_note-163] On 11 June 2023, three BYD showrooms and a service center in Hong Kong were vandalised. Suspects poured red paint on windows and entrances of the buildings. In one case, a car was rammed into the rolling shutter of the showroom in [New Territories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Territories).[^cite_note-164] The incidents were reported to the police, and operations in these premises were halted for a few days.[^cite_note-165] On 23 June 2023, Hong Kong police announced that 13 people were arrested in connection with the incidents.[^cite_note-166]

In June 2024, BYD started sales of the Denza brand in Hong Kong by introducing the [D9 EV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denza_D9) minivan.[^cite_note-167] It opened its first Denza flagship store in [Kowloon Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kowloon_Bay), which is the first presence of the brand outside mainland China.[^cite_note-168]

#### Southeast Asia

##### Thailand



BYD booth at the 2023 [Bangkok International Motor Show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangkok_International_Motor_Show), Thailand

In October 2022, BYD began selling passenger electric cars in Thailand through a partnership with local company Rêver Automotive.[^cite_note-169] In 2023, BYD announced plans to construct a new electric vehicle manufacturing plant in the [Eastern Economic Corridor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Economic_Corridor) (EEC) special zone in [Rayong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rayong), Thailand with an annual capacity of 150,000 vehicles. Construction of the plant began in March 2023.[^cite_note-170] The facility was officially opened on 4 July 2024, coinciding with the production of BYD's 8,000,000th new energy vehicle.[^cite_note-\:3-171] This plant is BYD's first wholly owned facility outside of China.[^cite_note-\:5-172] It handles stamping, painting, welding, final assembly, and car component production, and is set to employ 10,000 workers.[^cite_note-\:6-173] The company started selling Denza vehicles in Thailand since November 2024.[^cite_note-174] BYD's Thailand plant started exporting the BYD Dolphin to Europe in August 2025, in a bid to avoid import tariffs imposed to electric vehicles manufactured in China.[^cite_note-175]

##### Indonesia

BYD introduced its passenger electric cars in Indonesia in January 2024. According to the Indonesian government, BYD plans to invest US\$1.3 billion ([Rp ](367435.md)20.3 trillion) to build a manufacturing plant in Indonesia with an annual capacity of 150,000 units.[^cite_note-176] In April 2024, BYD formally announced the location of its Indonesian manufacturing plant, which will be in [Subang, West Java](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subang_Regency). The plant will be operational in early 2026.[^cite_note-177] BYD plans to produce its 15 millionth new energy vehicle in Indonesia.[^cite_note-178] In December 2024, BYD announced plans to increase its investment in the Subang facility by expanding the manufacturing plant area, increasing the projected workforce to 18,000 workers, and advancing the start of operations from early 2026.[^cite_note-179]

##### Malaysia

BYD entered Malaysia in December 2022 by partnering with local company [Sime Darby Motors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sime_Darby) as its distributor.[^cite_note-180][^cite_note-181] In August 2025, BYD announced plans to establish a [completely knocked down](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Completely_knocked_down) (CKD) assembly plant in Tanjong Malim, [Perak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Perak), with an investment of approximately RM1.3 billion and an annual production capacity of 50,000 vehicles. The facility was intended to begin operations in the second half of 2026.[^cite_note-NFT_2026-182]

On 29 September 2025, Malaysia's [Ministry of Investment, Trade and Industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Investment,_Trade_and_Industry) (MITI) issued BYD a temporary manufacturing licence, and the Perak state government subsequently fast-tracked land clearance and construction permits.[^cite_note-NFT_2026-182] However, by March 2026, it was reported that BYD was reconsidering its plans after being unable to agree to conditions set by MITI,[^cite_note-183][^cite_note-184] such as the stipulation that 80% of vehicles assembled at the plant would be required to be exported, and an initial localisation requirement of 40% for components sourced domestically.[^cite_note-185] MITI additionally imposed a minimum domestic sale price of RM100,000 for locally assembled vehicles to preserve market space for national manufacturers [Proton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proton_\(automobile\)) and [Perodua](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Perodua).[^cite_note-186] Construction at the Tanjong Malim site was subsequently halted, with the site reported to be inactive.[^cite_note-MM_2026-187] As of May 2026, negotiations between BYD and the Malaysian government remained ongoing, and no formal decision to cancel or proceed with the project had been announced.[^cite_note-MM_2026-187]

##### Singapore

BYD entered Singapore in 2014, initially supplying commercial electric vehicles including buses, light cargo vans, and trucks.[^cite_note-188] From 2019, the company expanded into the passenger car segment, partnering with Vantage Automotive Limited (VAL) as its authorised passenger car distributor, while commercial vehicle distribution was handled separately by [ST Engineering](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ST_Engineering) and [Inchcape](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inchcape_plc).[^cite_note-189][^cite_note-190]

BYD's passenger car sales grew substantially from 2022 onwards. In 2023, BYD registered 1,416 units in Singapore, entering the top 10 best-selling car brands for the first time.[^cite_note-191] In 2024, registrations rose to 6,191 units through authorised dealers, giving BYD a 14.4% market share and displacing long-time market leader Toyota.[^cite_note-192] For the full year 2025, BYD registered 11,184 units in Singapore, representing 21.2% of all new car registrations, retaining its position as the top-selling brand in the country for the second consecutive year.[^cite_note-193][^cite_note-194][^cite_note-195]

##### Philippines



A BYD dealership in [Quezon City](284223.md), Philippines

In the Philippines, BYD partnered with [Ayala Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayala_Corporation) to distribute BYD passenger electric cars in the country since August 2023.[^cite_note-196]

#### India

BYD entered the Indian automotive sector in 2016 as a battery and bus chassis supplier to Olectra Greenwich Ltd. Its manufacturing operations are based in the [Sriperumbudur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sriperumbudur) plant, Tamil Nadu. BYD started assembling electric passenger vehicles through a [semi-knock down](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Knock-down_kit) arrangement in low volume in 2022. The models assembled are the fleet-oriented [e6](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_e6) since September 2022 and the Atto 3 since November 2022.[^cite_note-197][^cite_note-198][^cite_note-The_Economic_Times-2022-199]

In July 2023, investment plans to produce cars in India were cancelled due to scrutiny from the Indian government, citing security concerns. BYD Auto had previously planned to invest US\$1 billion with a local joint venture partner, Megha Engineering, with production targeted to begin in 2025.[^cite_note-200]

#### Bangladesh

BYD entered the Bangladeshi market in partnership with [CG Runners](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Runner_Automobiles) by debuting their BYD Seal in March 2024.[^cite_note-201] Since then, they've been launching more car models such as the [Atto 3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Atto_3).[^cite_note-202]

In March 2026, [Runners Automobile PLC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Runner_Automobiles), in a disclosure to the [Dhaka Stock Exchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dhaka_Stock_Exchange), revealed that the company signed a deal with BYD to manufacture BYD EVs in Bangladesh at [Bhaluka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhaluka_Upazila), [Mymensingh District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mymensingh_District).[^cite_note-203][^cite_note-204]

#### Pakistan

BYD entered the Pakistani automotive market in August 2024 with the launch of the Atto 3, Seal, and Sealion 6 models. In addition, BYD has announced plans to establish a car production plant in [Port Qasim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_Qasim), [Karachi](17123.md) under a joint venture with Mega Motors, a subsidiary of [Hub Power Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hub_Power_Company), Pakistan's largest independent power producer. The facility is projected to be completed in the first half of 2026.[^cite_note-205][^cite_note-206]

#### Uzbekistan

BYD entered Uzbekistan in March 2023 by introducing two plug-in hybrid models and one battery electric model.[^cite_note-207] The company established a joint venture with the local company Uzavtosanoat JSC ([UzAuto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UzAuto_Motors)) to assemble plug-in hybrid cars starting in 2024. The plant is located in [Jizzakh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jizzakh).[^cite_note-208] The first vehicle produced by the facility, a [BYD Song Plus DM-i](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Song_Plus), rolled off the assembly line on 27 June 2024.[^cite_note-Zhang_2024-209]

### Europe



BYD booth at the [IAA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Motor_Show_Germany) Summit 2023, [Munich](19058.md), Germany

In May 2020, BYD Auto announced that it would offer passenger vehicles in Europe, starting with [Norway](21241.md). The company chose Norway due to the widespread adoption of electric vehicles in the country. The first product offered there is the [BYD Tang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Tang).[^cite_note-210] Previous BYD vehicles offered in Europe are mainly commercial vehicles and fleet-oriented vehicles such as the [BYD e6](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_e6), sold in low volumes.[^cite_note-211]

In 2022, BYD Auto began selling its passenger vehicles in more European countries, such as [Denmark](76972.md), [Sweden](5058739.md), the [Netherlands](21148.md), [Germany](11867.md), [France](5843419.md), and [Belgium](3343.md).[^cite_note-212] In Germany and [Sweden](5058739.md), the BYD models are sold in cooperation with the Hedin Group.[^cite_note-Randall-2022-213] In October 2022, BYD appointed Denzel Group as its distributor in [Austria](26964606.md).[^cite_note-Randall-2022-213] In March 2023, BYD entered the [United Kingdom](31717.md) market by introducing the Atto 3.[^cite_note-214] In June 2024, BYD began selling three BEV passenger car models in [Poland](22936.md).[^cite_note-215]

BYD built its first European passenger car factory in [Szeged](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Szeged), Hungary, which will build new energy vehicles with an annual capacity of over 100,000 vehicles in 2026. The plan was first announced in December 2023.[^cite_note-216][^cite_note-217] It is the first Chinese manufacturer to plan a European factory.[^cite_note-218]

In September 2023, the [European Commission](9974.md) announced [EU](9317.md) would launch an anti-subsidy investigation into Chinese electric vehicle manufacturers, including BYD. European Commission president [Ursula von der Leyen](2991932.md) claimed that Chinese electric vehicle prices are kept artificially low by significant state subsidies, that would in effect distort the EU market.[^cite_note-219] BYD Europe managing director, Michael Shu, argued that BYD's competitive price was achieved by higher "management efficiency" and "unique technology" instead of subsidies from the Chinese government.[^cite_note-220] In June 2024, the European Commission completed its investigation, and imposed an additional 17.4% [import duty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Import_duty) on top of the existing 15% on BYD vehicles imported into the [European Union](9317.md), effective 4 July 2024.[^cite_note-221][^cite_note-222]

BYD vehicles generate significantly higher profitability in Europe than in China, driven by their higher pricing in the European market. Research by the Rhodium Group shows that BYD sells their vehicles in Europe with a 92–112% markup compared to its price in China. As the result, BYD makes approximately €14,300 in profit on each [Seal U EV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Seal_U) model sold in the EU, which is an 11-fold increase compared to the €1,300 profit on the same model sold in China. *[Reuters](18998750.md)* noted that the BYD Atto 3 is priced at US\$19,283 in China and US\$42,789 in Germany. Despite the higher price in Germany, it remains competitive with similar electric vehicles there. The price difference can be attributed to the more competitive market conditions in China, while in Europe, BYD is able to position its vehicles at a higher price point. This indicates that despite the tariffs imposed by the EU, BYD is likely able to continue create profit in Europe.[^cite_note-223][^cite_note-224][^cite_note-225]

In April 2025, BYD's EV sales in Europe surpassed that of Tesla, which was the leading seller on the European EV market for several years.[^cite_note-226]

#### Turkey

BYD announced plans to invest \$1 billion in a factory in Turkey, which is part of a customs union with the EU. The factory will have an annual production capacity of 150,000 vehicles. An agreement between BYD CEO Wang Chuanfu and Turkey's [Industry and Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Industry_and_Technology) Minister [Mehmet Fatih Kacır](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mehmet_Fatih_Kac%C4%B1r) was signed on 8 July 2024.[^cite_note-227]

### North America

#### United States



BYD Auto former US headquarters in [Los Angeles, California](18110.md) in 2015

BYD's North American headquarters were opened in [Los Angeles](18110.md) in 2011.[^cite_note-228] The electric bus plant in [Lancaster, California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lancaster,_California) became operational in 2014.[^cite_note-Martinez-2022-229][^cite_note-Kane-2019-230] BYD first supplied the [Los Angeles Metro Bus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Metro_Bus) system with buses in 2015. This unit was spun off as **RIDE Mobility** in 2023.

In a February 2024 interview with [Yahoo! Finance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yahoo!_Finance), Stella Li, the executive vice president of BYD and CEO of BYD Americas iterated that BYD is "not planning to come to the US" to sell electric passenger cars, despite planning a manufacturing plant in Mexico, citing politically motivated [trade barriers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trade_barrier) against Chinese companies and the slowing rate of growth for [electric car adoption](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electric_car_use_by_country) in the US.[^cite_note-Yahoo_Finance-2024-231] BYD is one of the top electric bus manufacturers in North America, and has been primarily adopted by the metro transit authorities of Los Angeles and San Francisco.[^cite_note-Martinez-2022-229]

#### Mexico

BYD started selling electric passenger cars in Mexico since 2023. The first models introduced were the Han EV sedan, Tang EV and [Yuan Plus EV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Yuan_Plus).[^cite_note-232][^cite_note-233] Previously the company has presence in the country selling battery electric buses, trucks and taxis.[^cite_note-234] Reports in February 2024 indicated that BYD is planning to build a manufacturing plant in Mexico. According to Stella Li, the company is not considering any northern state as it targets the local market instead of the US market.[^cite_note-Yahoo_Finance-2024-231]

#### Canada

BYD is expanding into the Canadian market under a trade agreement that replaced its initial 100% surtax on Chinese electric EVs with an annual quota of just 49,000 Chinese-made EVs. Following the reduction of Canadian tariffs on Chinese electric vehicles, BYD plans to open 20 dealerships in Canada starting in 2027, targeting metropolitan markets such as Toronto, Montreal, Vancouver, and Calgary.[^cite_note-235][^cite_note-236]

### Oceania

#### Australia



A BYD dealership in [Perth](24355.md), Western Australia

BYD entered the Australian market in 2022 through a partnership with EVDirect, a local distributor that is a subsidiary of [ASX](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Securities_Exchange)-listed company MotorCycle Holdings. Both companies signed an agreement in February 2021.[^cite_note-237] The company showcased the first [right-hand drive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Right-hand_drive) Atto 3 in August 2022.[^cite_note-238] Its entry into the Australian market was delayed by a month to November 2022 due to compliance issues with the Atto 3, due to the top tether [child restraint](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Child_safety_seat) anchor point in the center rear seating position not being in compliance with the [Australian Design Rules](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Design_Rules).[^cite_note-239] Despite the delay, the Atto 3 became the second-best-selling electric vehicle in Australia in 2022, behind only the [Tesla Model 3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tesla_Model_3).[^cite_note-240] In 2023, BYD introduced two more models (the Dolphin and the Seal) in Australia, and by the end of the year has sold 12,438 vehicles, making it the second-largest electric vehicle brand in the country.[^cite_note-241] In 2024, the brand introduced two plug-in hybrid vehicles in the country, starting with the Sealion 6 in May, and the [Shark 6](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Shark_6) in October.[^cite_note-242][^cite_note-243]

In May 2025, BYD announced that it will assume direct control of its operations in Australia beginning July 2025, ending EVDirect's role as the brand's local distributor. EVDirect will retain involvement with BYD through a minority shareholding in EVDealer Group, a retail joint venture with Eagers Automotive. The arrangement maintains the current retail and service network, and enables Eagers Automotive to expand BYD's retail presence in Australia.[^cite_note-244]

### Central and South America



BYD’s Camaçari factory during an official visit attended by Brazil's President [Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva](99328.md), BYD chairman [Wang Chuanfu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wang_Chuanfu), and BYD vice president [Stella Li](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stella_Li), October 2025

BYD planned to deliver 1,002 electric buses to [Bogotá](211271.md), the capital city of [Colombia](5222.md), by mid-2022,[^cite_note-245] after winning a contract for 406 electric buses in January 2021.[^cite_note-246]

In 2022, BYD began selling passenger vehicles in Colombia and Costa Rica.[^cite_note-The_Economic_Times-2022-199]

BYD began operations in El Salvador in September 2024 through a partnership with Energy Motors, launching its first showroom in San Salvador at Centro Comercial Galerías. The company introduced a lineup of electric and plug-in hybrid vehicles, with plans to expand to San Miguel and Santa Ana in 2025.[^cite_note-elsalvador.com-2025-247]

#### Brazil

In February 2022, BYD Auto began sales of passenger vehicles in Brazil starting with the Tang EV (marketed as the Tan), followed by the Han EV in April 2022.[^cite_note-248][^cite_note-249] Previously, BYD in Brazil had marketed battery electric buses and commercial vehicles.[^cite_note-250]



BYD Dolphin [taxi](22026933.md) in [Salvador](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salvador,_Bahia), Brazil.

In July 2023, BYD announced an investment of US\$600 million in Brazil to acquire, modernise, and increase the production capacity of a former [Ford](30433662.md) manufacturing plant in [Camaçari, Bahia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cama%C3%A7ari,_Bahia), to build up to 300,000 cars per year by 2025. The plant will produce the Dolphin, Yuan Plus, and Song Plus DM-i.[^cite_note-251] The construction of this factory was however stopped before Christmas 2024 as the Brazilian Ministry of Work accused the construction company to flout labour laws and hold their workers in "slavery-like conditions".[^cite_note-252]

BYD also plans to build two industrial plants: one for the production of electric bus/truck platforms and another for refining [lithium](17561.md) and [iron phosphate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iron_phosphate) ores for use in BYD's battery factories in China.[^cite_note-253] In June 2024, BYD announced its goal to sell 350,000 vehicles annually in Brazil by 2028 and to become one of the top three car brands in the country.[^cite_note-254]

## Products

Main article: [List of BYD Auto vehicles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_BYD_Auto_vehicles)



A BYD Dynasty Network showroom in [Shenzhen](83368.md)

BYD Auto offers a broad variety of vehicle types, including sedans, hatchbacks, [MPVs](20828.md) and [SUVs](47696.md) across four different brands. It also produces [commercial vehicles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commercial_vehicles) such as battery electric buses and coaches in various sizes, and large trucks. The company formerly produced [internal combustion engine](41228673.md) (ICE) vehicles until 2022, when the carmaker announced that it would focus on new energy vehicles.[^cite_note-255]

For passenger vehicles for personal use, the BYD brand distinguishes its line-up under three main *series*: the Dynasty Series (started by the [Qin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Qin) in 2012), Ocean Series (started by the [Dolphin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Dolphin) in 2021), and the *e* Series (started by the [e6](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_e6) in 2009).[^cite_note-Kuhudzai-2023-257] In China, these series are offered through separate dealership chains, namely the Dynasty Network ([Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simplified_Chinese_characters): 王朝网; [pinyin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pinyin): *Wángcháo Wǎng*) and the Ocean Network ([Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simplified_Chinese_characters): 海洋网; [pinyin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pinyin): *Hǎiyáng Wǎng*) (formerly the e-Network in 2019–2021).[^cite_note-258][^cite_note-259] e Series vehicles are distributed through the Ocean Network.[^cite_note-\:12-260]

Dynasty Series vehicles are designed with [dragon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dragon)-inspired elements, and targets older customers. The nameplates used include [Xia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Xia), [Han](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Han), [Tang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Tang), [Qin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Qin), [Song](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Song), and [Yuan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Yuan).[^cite_note-261][^cite_note-262] Ocean Series vehicles are designed with a concept of "Ocean Aesthetics", using use of elements such as waves and flowing lines, and positioned to appeal to younger customers. Ocean Series vehicles predominantly uses marine animal names such as Seagull, Dolphin, [Seal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Seal_\(nameplate\)) and [Sealion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Sealion), except two models that are named after [warships](263960.md) (e.g. [Frigate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Frigate_07) and [Destroyer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Destroyer_05)). BYD started phasing out the warship-named vehicles in 2025, merging both models into the Sealion and Seal nameplate instead.[^cite_note-263] [Fleet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fleet_vehicle)-oriented vehicles for [ride-hailing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ride-hailing) and taxi use are categorised in the "e" series.[^cite_note-\:12-260]

BYD passenger vehicles consist of [battery electric vehicles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battery_electric_vehicle), denoted by the EV moniker, and [plug-in hybrids](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plug-in_hybrid) marketed as DM-i (Dual Mode Intelligent, a [plug-in hybrid electric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plug-in_hybrid) system) and DM-p (performance-oriented Dual Mode with [all-wheel drive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All-wheel_drive)).[^cite_note-Kuhudzai-2023-257]

#### Brands

##### Fangchengbao

Main article: [Fangchengbao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fangchengbao)

Fangchengbao ([Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_language): 方程豹; lit. 'formula leopard'), [trademarked](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trademark) in English as Formula Bao[^cite_note-264] is BYD Auto's brand that produces SUVs. BYD positioned the brand with "professional" and "personalised" vehicles, with products covering [off-road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Off-road_vehicle) and [track](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Track_racing)-focused segments. The brand was announced on 9 June 2023.[^cite_note-265]

On 16 August 2023, Fangchengbao released its first model, the [Bao 5](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fangchengbao_Bao_5) plug-in hybrid SUV. It debuted at the Chengdu Auto Show and is based on a Fangchengbao-specific platform called DMO (Dual Mode Off-Road).[^cite_note-Opletal-2023-266]

##### Denza

Main article: [Denza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denza)

Denza ([Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_language): 腾势; [pinyin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pinyin): *Téngshì*) is BYD's first [luxury car](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luxury_car) brand.[^cite_note-267] It was established in May 2010 as a [joint venture](538782.md) with [Mercedes-Benz Group](42977.md) (previously Daimler AG).[^cite_note-bleak-268] Its first model, the [Denza 500](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denza_500) is based on an earlier generation of the [Mercedes-Benz B-Class](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercedes-Benz_B-Class).[^cite_note-269] After years of low sales, in 2021 Daimler reduced its share from 50% to 10%, leaving BYD Auto Industry Co., Ltd. as the largest shareholder at 90%.[^cite_note-Zhang/CnEVPost-2021-86] The brand line-up was refreshed with the release of the [Denza D9](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denza_D9) minivan in 2022, followed by the [Denza N7](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denza_N7) SUV.[^cite_note-270] In September 2024, Mercedes-Benz withdrew from the joint venture by transferring its remaining 10% stake to BYD, making Denza a wholly owned brand of BYD.[^cite_note-\:03-63]

#### Linghui

Main article: [Linghui](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Linghui)

Linghui ([Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simplified_Chinese_characters): 领汇汽车) is BYD's fifth brand, established specifically to serve the [taxi](22026933.md) and [ride-hailing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ride-hailing) markets. The marque was created to strategically separate BYD's fleet operations from its consumer vehicles. Linghui operates independently from the main brand, featuring its own dedicated logo and distinct sales channels. Linghui's initial product lineup consists entirely of rebadged existing BYD models.[^cite_note-273][^cite_note-274]

### Commercial vehicles

#### Light commercial vehicles and taxis

The [BYD e6](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_e6), first introduced in 2009 as the first battery electric vehicle from BYD, is predominantly marketed as a taxi. It has been used as a taxi in many cities in China, Europe, and other Asian countries, as well as in fleets for [ride-sharing apps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ridesharing_company).[^cite_note-275] BYD also offers battery electric panel [vans](63679.md) and windowed vans such as the [T3/ETP3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_M3) and [V3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_V3), and a plug-in hybrid pickup truck, the [BYD Shark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Shark).[^cite_note-276][^cite_note-277]

#### Buses

BYD produces battery electric buses in various forms and sizes, such as single-deck buses, [double-deck buses](902183.md), [articulated buses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Articulated_buses), [school buses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/School_buses), and long-distance [coaches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coach_\(bus\)).[^cite_note-278][^cite_note-279][^cite_note-280] As of 2023, BYD has delivered over 100,000 battery electric buses globally.[^cite_note-281]

&#x20;In North America, BYD buses are produced in the US at BYD's plant in [Lancaster, California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lancaster,_California). As of 2022, it is the largest battery-electric bus manufacturer in North America.[^cite_note-Martinez-2022-229]

#### Trucks

&#x20;BYD is one of the largest battery electric truck manufacturers in the world, with more than 8,000 trucks in service globally as of 2021. It has sold more than 200 battery electric trucks in service across the United States.[^cite_note-282] It produces [Class 5, Class 6, Class 7 and Class 8](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Truck_classification) trucks, [refuse trucks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Refuse_truck), [street sweeper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Street_sweeper) trucks, [mining trucks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mining_truck), and construction vehicles.[^cite_note-283][^cite_note-284][^cite_note-285]
