## Technologies

### Blade battery

Main article: [BYD Blade battery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Blade_battery)



The [Blade battery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blade_battery) showcased at the [IAA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Motor_Show_Germany) Summit 2023, Germany

The Blade battery is a [lithium iron phosphate (LFP) battery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithium_iron_phosphate_battery) for electric vehicles designed and manufactured by [FinDreams Battery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FinDreams_Battery), a subsidiary of BYD Company. It was first used by the [BYD Han](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Han) in 2020.[^cite_note-Kane-2020-79] Starting from April 2021, every battery electric vehicle from BYD comes equipped with the Blade battery.[^cite_note-286] The battery is claimed to be thinner and safer than typical electric vehicle batteries, and has been used by other car companies.[^cite_note-287][^cite_note-electrek20210805-288] BYD claims that in a penetration test where the battery is impaled with a nail, the Blade battery emitted no smoke or fire after being penetrated, and its surface temperature reached only 30 to 60 °C (86 to 140 °F). In the same test, a three-layered [lithium battery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithium-ion_battery) would heat up to over 500 °C (932 °F) and subsequently catch fire.[^cite_note-289]

### Plug-in hybrid electric systems

#### &#x20;DM (dual mode)

The "DM" or "dual mode" is BYD's term for its [plug-in hybrid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plug-in_hybrid) [drivetrain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hybrid_vehicle_drivetrain) technology that was first introduced in 2008 on its early hybrid vehicles such as the [BYD F3DM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_F3DM) and [BYD M3 DM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_M3_DM). It consists of a conventional internal combustion engine and transmission as well as an electric motor and batteries.[^cite_note-290] The first generation DM system is considered a [series-parallel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Series-parallel_hybrid) drivetrain. It uses an [E-CVT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transmission_\(mechanics\)#Electric_variable), which is a power unit integrating a drive motor, a [generator](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electric_generator) and a reducer, and does not have a multi-speed transmission in the traditional sense. The petrol engine drives the generator to charge the battery and power the electric motor, and is able to supply power to the wheels.[^cite_note-\:1-291]

In 2013, BYD launched the second-generation DM technology (also called DM2.0). This system is more performance-oriented, and consists of a [water-cooled](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water-cooled) electric motor with a six-speed [dual-clutch transmission (DCT)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dual-clutch_transmission) ("P3") and a 1.5-litre [turbocharged](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turbocharged) four-cylinder [direct injection petrol engine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gasoline_direct_injection) to form a [parallel hybrid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parallel_hybrid) drivetrain. It has three driving modes, which are EV, HEV and ICE.[^cite_note-\:1-291] This technology was used by the first generations of the [BYD Qin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Qin), [BYD Song](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Song), [BYD Tang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Tang) and other models. BYD also offered an all-wheel drive version by adding a drive motor ("P4") to the rear axle.[^cite_note-\:2-292] The third-generation DM system (also called DM3.0) brought improved performance and smoother shifting by adding a [belt-driven starter generator (BSG)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BAS_hybrid).[^cite_note-\:1-291]

#### &#x20;DM-i/DM-p/DMO (fourth generation)



BYD DM-i powertrain on display

In 2021, BYD introduced the DM-i and DM-p systems that replaced the outgoing DM systems.\[^cite_note-\:2-292] The first models that uses the DM-i system are the Qin Plus DM-i, Song Plus DM-i and Tang DM-i, all released in February 2021.[^cite_note-293] The DM-i was developed for efficiency-oriented [front-wheel drive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Front-wheel_drive) models, while the DM-p is adopted for performance-oriented [all-wheel drive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All-wheel_drive) models. These systems adopts [series-parallel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Series-parallel_hybrid) [plug-in hybrid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plug-in_hybrid) technology with a dedicated hybrid transmission (DHT),[^cite_note-294] consisting of a high-efficiency petrol engine marketed as Xiaoyun (which can power an [electric generator](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electric_generator)) and a main [electric motor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electric_motor) (which can be powered by batteries and/or an electric generator). The Xiaoyun engine uses the [Atkinson cycle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atkinson_cycle), has an ultra-high [compression ratio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Compression_ratio) (CR) of 15.5, and has a [brake thermal efficiency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thermal_efficiency) (BTE) of 43%, which is claimed to be the world's highest for a production petrol engine.\[^cite_note-[www.businesswire.com-2020-295](http://www.businesswire.com-2020-295/)] The DM-i is available with three power levels, codenamed EHS132, EHS145, and EHS160, using a 1.5-litre, a 1.5-litre or a 1.5-litre [turbocharged](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turbocharged) Xiaoyun engines respectively.[^cite_note-296]

The hybrid drivetrain uses context-dependent components to provide driving power, including battery-only, battery + electrical generator mode, engine-only mode (only at high speeds due to lack of a multiple-geared transmission), and battery + engine mode. The engine charges the batteries via the generator when conditions permit. This design allows the engine to remain within its high-efficiency zone.\[^cite_note-[www.businesswire.com-2020-295](http://www.businesswire.com-2020-295/)]

In late 2023, a derivative of the DM-i and DM-p fourth-generation systems called DMO (Dual Mode Off-road), aka DMO Super Hybrid Off-road Platform[^cite_note-297] was introduced. It is developed for [body-on-frame](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Body-on-frame), [off-road oriented vehicles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Off-road_vehicle) that use a [longitudinal engine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Longitudinal_engine) layout such as the [Fangchengbao Bao 5](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fangchengbao_Bao_5) SUV and the [BYD Shark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Shark) pickup truck.[^cite_note-298]

In January 2024, BYD plans to end the production of pouch-type batteries used in its plug-in hybrid vehicles by 2025 to address durability concerns and the risk of leaking [electrolyte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electrolyte). Instead, the company will use prismatic batteries known as "Short Blade" for its plug-in hybrid vehicles going forward.[^cite_note-299]

#### &#x20;Fifth generation DM-i

The fifth-generation DM-i system (DM-i 5.0) was introduced in May 2024 with the introduction of the [BYD Qin L](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Qin_L) and [BYD Seal 06 DM-i](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Seal_06_DM-i). The system is claimed to have the world's highest engine thermal efficiency of 46.06%, the world's lowest fuel consumption at 2.9 l/100 km (34 km/l; 81 mpg<sub>‑US</sub>), and the world's longest combined range of 2,100 km (1,300 mi).[^cite_note-\:2-292]

### Battery electric vehicle platforms

#### e-Platform

The e-Platform, or retroactively known as the e-Platform 1.0 was launched in 2010. It was first adopted by the first-generation [BYD e6](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_e6).[^cite_note-300][^cite_note-\:4-301]

#### e-Platform 2.0

The **e-Platform 2.0** is a [modular](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modularity) [car platform](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Car_platform) specialised for [battery electric vehicles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battery_electric_vehicle). It was introduced in 2018, and went on production with the [BYD Han EV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Han) in 2020. The platform is summarised by the "33111" concept: the first "3" refers to the three-in-one drive system, which integrates the motor, motor controller, and reducer; the second "3" refers to the 3-in-1 high-voltage system, encompassing the [DC-DC converter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DC-DC_converter), on-board charger, and high-voltage distribution box. The three "1"s represent the integrated [printed circuit board](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Printed_circuit_board), the smart [infotainment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/In-car_entertainment) screen, and the high-performance and safer battery.[^cite_note-\:4-301]

#### e-Platform 3.0



The e-Platform 3.0 on display

The **e-Platform 3.0** is a modular car platform specialised for battery electric vehicles. It offers improved integration of BYD's proprietary Blade battery technology into an improved pure electric frame with doubled torsional stiffness, a more efficient 8-in-1 module for the drive system, a direct cooling and heating system for the [battery pack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electric_vehicle_battery) (utilising [residual heat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waste_heat), the [powertrain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Powertrain), the passenger compartment, and the battery itself) for increased [thermal efficiency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thermal_efficiency) of up to 20%, as well as shorter front [overhangs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Overhang_\(automotive\)), a lower [body profile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Car_body_style), and a longer [wheelbase](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wheelbase) to improve [aerodynamics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aerodynamics). The platform enables [all-electric ranges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All-electric_range) exceeding 1,000 [km](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kilometre) (620 [mi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mile)), with 800-[volt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volt) fast charging technology for a range of up to 150 km (93 mi) after 5 minutes of charging and allows for an [all-wheel drive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All_wheel_drive) (AWD) system with [0-100 km/h (62 mph)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/0_to_60_mph) acceleration in as fast as 2.9 seconds.[^cite_note-Rahman-2021-302] It allows for cell-to-body (CTB) battery integration, replacing the outgoing cell-to-pack (CTP) technology.[^cite_note-303]

The first vehicles designed using this platform are the [Dolphin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Dolphin), Yuan Plus ([Atto 3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Atto_3) in overseas markets),[^cite_note-304] [Seal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Seal_\(2022\)), [Denza D9 EV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denza_D9), among others.[^cite_note-Kane-2021-83]

##### e-Platform 3.0 Evo

In 2024, BYD introduced an improved version of the platform called the e-Platform 3.0 Evo. It features a 12-in-1 electric drive system, replacing the previous 8-in-1 system. It also features the world highest speed mass-produced electric drive motor reaching 23,000 rpm, faster charging speed, and a high-efficiency [heat pump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heat_pump).[^cite_note-305] The first vehicle that uses this platform is the [BYD Sealion 07 EV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Sealion_07_EV).[^cite_note-306]

#### Super e-Platform

The Super e-Platform is BYD's latest battery electric platform with an overhaul in [propulsion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Propulsion), power electronics, and battery architecture. The platform incorporates a 1000 V electrical system, supports a maximum charging power of 1,000 kW (1 MW), and introduces advancements in motor speed, [power density](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Power_density), and [semiconductor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Semiconductor) components. The platform also supports dual-gun charging configurations.[^cite_note-307]

The Super e-Platform includes the first mass-produced 30,000 rpm electric [traction motor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traction_motor), that achieves a peak output of 580 kW (778 hp; 789 PS) and a maximum [rotational speed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rotational_speed) of 30,511 [revolutions per minute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revolutions_per_minute). The increase in rotational speed allows for a more compact and lightweight design, and enabling higher vehicle top speeds exceeding 300 km/h (190 mph).[^cite_note-308] The motor's power density is rated at 16.4 kW/kg. To support such power output and rotational speed, the platform integrates a new generation of [silicon carbide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silicon_carbide) (SiC) power semiconductors with a voltage rating of up to 1500 V. The SiC devices contribute to [thermal management](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thermal_management_\(electronics\)) efficiency and extend operational voltage margins, which are necessary for 1000 V architectures.[^cite_note-309]

The battery system used in the Super e-Platform consists of reengineered [blade cells](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blade_battery) labeled as Flash Charging Battery. These cells support a 10C charging rate, a current of up to 1000 A, and voltage operation up to 1000 V, enabling a peak charging power of 1,000 kW (1 MW).[^cite_note-310]

The first production models utilizing this platform are the [BYD Han L EV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Han_L) and [BYD Tang L EV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Tang_L). These vehicles feature acceleration times of 0–100 km/h (0–62 mph) as fast as 2.7 seconds, depending on configuration.[^cite_note-311][^cite_note-312]

### Fast charging systems

#### 1,000 kW fast charging (2025)



BYD's 1,000 kW (1 MW) charging station

In March 2025, BYD introduced a 1,000 kW fast charging system as part of its Super e-Platform. The system operates at 1,000 volts and supports charging currents up to 1,000 amperes, delivering a peak charging power of 1,000 kW (1 MW). It uses a revised version of BYD's Blade battery with a 10C charging rate, which enables approximately 400 km (250 mi) of range to be added in five minutes with an approximate rate of 2 km (1.2 mi) of range per second. The battery improvements include a new [electrolyte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electrolyte) formulation and a modified separator designed to support high current flow and rapid charge acceptance without overheating or degradation.

The charging system includes [liquid-cooled](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liquid-cooled) charging cables to manage heat generated during high-power transfers. To mitigate stress on the electrical grid, the charging stations are equipped with integrated [energy storage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_storage) systems, which act as buffers to supply power during peak demand or in locations with limited grid capacity.[^cite_note-313][^cite_note-314]

BYD plans to deploy over 4,000 of these 1 MW chargers across China, with around 500 stations expected to be operational in the initial rollout phase. These stations are designed to be compatible with upcoming BYD models, beginning with the [BYD Han L](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Han_L) and [BYD Tang L](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Tang_L), which are the first vehicles configured to utilize the full capabilities of the 1 MW infrastructure.[^cite_note-315]

#### 1,500 kW Flash Charging and second-generation Blade battery (2026)

Main article: [BYD Flash Charging](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Flash_Charging)





BYD's Flash Charging station with its T-shaped design

In March 2026, BYD introduced its second-generation Blade battery alongside an ultra-fast "Flash Charging" ([Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simplified_Chinese_characters): 闪充; [pinyin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pinyin): *Shǎnchōng*) technology. The updated battery architecture is claimed to feature higher [energy density](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_density), an extended operational lifespan, and improved safety standards compared to the first generation. It enables highly rapid charging, allowing a compatible vehicle to charge from a 10% to 70% state of charge in 5 minutes, and from 10% to 97% in 9 minutes. The battery's thermal management system is designed to maintain rapid charging capabilities in extreme cold environments; at temperatures of -20 °C (-4 °F), the battery can charge from 20% to 97% in 12 minutes. At -30 °C (-22 °F), charging times are extended by only 3 minutes compared to standard room temperature rates.[^cite_note-\:19-108]

To support the second-generation Blade battery, BYD simultaneously deployed a new generation of Flash Charging stations capable of a maximum single-gun output power of 1,500 kW (1.5 MW) operating on a 1,000 V high-voltage system.[^cite_note-\:16-316]

### Automobiles semiconductor

#### Xuanji A3

On 28 May 2026, at the intelligent strategy launch conference held by BYD, Wang Chuanfu, Chairman and President of BYD Company, released "**Xuanji A3**" ([Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simplified_Chinese_characters): 璇玑A3), BYD's 567th automobile-grade chip and first in-house developed assisted driving chip, Also is China's first automotive-grade 4 nm assisted driving chip.[^cite_note-317][^cite_note-\:Xuanji_A3-318]

The Xuanji A3 was entirely developed, designed, and tested by BYD Semiconductor. Its CPU has 16 cores, power consumption per unit of computing power is 20% lower, and it supports L3 and L4 level autonomous driving. The computing power of the whole vehicle (3 chips) exceeds 2100 TOPS. Combined with self-developed algorithms for deep optimization, the computing power utilization rate is improved by 100%. The chip has started mass production.[^cite_note-319]

### Driver assistance

In April 2020, BYD introduced **DiPilot**, a branding it uses for its [advanced driver-assistance system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Advanced_driver-assistance_system) (ADAS). It debuted with the [BYD Han](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Han). DiPilot consisted of DiTrainer, a big data algorithm that can learn the driver's driving habits, and DiDAS, the core ADAS feature.[^cite_note-320]

In September 2024, BYD established an advanced technology R\&D center to support smart driving systems, smart cockpits, dual-mode technology, and other departments related to computing power, algorithms, and large models.[^cite_note-321] The company claims it has a team of 5,000 R\&D engineers solely working on driver assistance technologies.[^cite_note-322]

#### TianShen



Demonstration of BYD TianShen C

BYD's latest proprietary [driver assistance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Driver_assistance)/[self-driving](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Self-driving_car) system is named **TianShen** ([Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simplified_Chinese_characters): 天神之眼; lit. 'God's Eye', alternatively 'Divine Eye'[^cite_note-323]), which enables driver-supervised automation in tasks such as overtaking and parking. Categorised as "[Level 2+](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Self-driving_car#Classifications)" automation (except the Level 3 TianShen A), BYD claimed the TianShen system can accomplish "over 1,000 kilometers of autonomous driving without intervention and has a 99% success rate for automated [valet parking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valet_parking)". TianShen is planned for incorporation on almost all models of BYD vehicles.[^cite_note-324][^cite_note-325][^cite_note-326]

The first model to feature the TianShen system is the [Denza N7](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denza_N7), which was introduced in July 2023.[^cite_note-327] During its introduction, the Denza N7 can be optioned with two different TianShen system.[^cite_note-328] The advanced version uses [Nvidia](39120.md) Orin X 254 trillion operations per second (TOPS) [processor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Processor_\(computing\)) and a more advanced [radar](25676.md), or a [Qualcomm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qualcomm) chip in the standard option.[^cite_note-329]

The TianShen A and B versions are provided by Chinese autonomous driving solution company [Momenta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Momenta), while the C version is developed in-house by BYD.[^cite_note-330]

In February 2025, BYD announced that the system became fitted as standard to 21 of BYD's 30 cars split across four brands, including its cheapest and smallest vehicle, the BYD Seagull.[^cite_note-331] As of 2025, the "TianShen" system is available in three tiers, each designed for different vehicle models and price ranges, which are:

- **TianShen A:** The most advanced version, used by [Yangwang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yangwang) vehicles. Equipped with three LIDAR sensors, and is Level 3 highway and city capable.

  [^cite_note-332]

  &#x20;Powered by the DiPilot 600 system with two Nvidia Orin X chips, supporting up to 600 TOPS of computing power. Supports NOA (Navigation on Autopilot) function in cities and highways.

  [^cite_note-\:9-333]

- **TianShen B:** Used on Denza and BYD vehicles priced above 200,000 RMB. Equipped with an additional [LIDAR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LIDAR) sensor mounted on the roof of the vehicle. Powered by the DiPilot 300 system with a single Nvidia Orin X chip, supporting up to 300 TOPS of computing power. Supports NOA (Navigation on Autopilot) function in cities and highways.

  [^cite_note-\:9-333]

  [^cite_note-334]

- **TianShen C:** The most basic version, used on all mainstream BYD vehicles priced under 200,000 [RMB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RMB). Includes 12 [cameras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Onboard_camera) (three [front-view](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dashcam) behind the [windshield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Windshield), five panoramic, and four [surround-view](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surround-view_system)), five [mmWave radars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mmwave_sensing) for 360-degree perception, and 12 [ultrasonic radar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doppler_radar) sensors. Powered by the DiPilot 100 system with either a single Nvidia Drive Orin N chip or [Horizon Robotics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Horizon_Robotics) Journey 5 chip, supporting up to 100 [trillion operations per second](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operations_per_second#Floating-point_arithmetic) (TOPS) of [computing power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Computing_power). Supports high-speed NOA (Navigation on Autopilot) function in highways.

  [^cite_note-\:9-333]

Every BYD car equipped with the TianShen system will also feature the Xuanji architecture, which was launched in January 2024.[^cite_note-335] The architecture includes a central processor, cloud [AI](1164.md), vehicle-side AI, [Internet of Vehicles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internet_of_Vehicles), [5G network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/5G), [satellite network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Satellite_network), sensors chain, control chain, data chain, and mechanical chain. The architecture will also be integrated with the [DeepSeek R1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DeepSeek) [large model](73248112.md) to enhance AI capabilities in both the vehicle and the cloud.[^cite_note-\:9-333]

In some models such as the [Fangchengbao Bao 5](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fangchengbao_Bao_5), BYD offers the TianShen C driving assistance system alongside [Huawei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Huawei)'s more advanced solution, marketed as Qiankun ADS 3.0, that uses single 192-channel lidar.[^cite_note-336]

### Software and connectivity

BYD equipped most of its passenger cars with **DiLink**, a service ecosystem of technology and content developed independently by BYD and is primarily based on [Android](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Android_OS). The system is claimed to integrate in-vehicle network systems, [cloud](19541494.md) communication, [AI](1164.md), [big data](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_data) and other technologies.[^cite_note-337] The [user interface](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/User_interface) is inspired by mobile phones, supported by rotatable screens in BYD vehicles, and supports [over-the-air updates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Over-the-air_updates). BYD also cooperated with [Alibaba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alibaba_Group) Cloud for its DiCloud AI cloud platform for the DiLink.[^cite_note-338] Initial iterations of the DiLink (version 1.0) were used in vehicles such as the [second-generation BYD Tang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Tang#II), and uses [hardware](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Computer_hardware) comprising a 14.6-inch screen, [4G](486547.md) connection and a [Qualcomm Snapdragon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qualcomm_Snapdragon) 625 [chipset](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chipset) with 3 [GB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gigabyte) of [RAM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Random-access_memory) and 32 GB of [storage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Computer_data_storage).[^cite_note-339]

The DiLink 4.0 was introduced in August 2021. The updated version supports [5G](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/5G) network, and feature a reworked, more intuitive user interface.[^cite_note-340] The hardware for the DiLink 4.0 is known to be powered by a Qualcomm Snapdragon 690 chipset with 8 GB of RAM and 128 GB of storage.[^cite_note-341]

One of the popular feature in the DiLink for the Chinese market is the inclusion of [karaoke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karaoke). BYD is one of the first brands in China to include karaoke in the [in-car entertainment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/In-car_entertainment) system, allowing occupants to sing through the optional or included DiLink [microphone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Microphone).[^cite_note-342][^cite_note-343][^cite_note-344]

In 2021, BYD also introduced BYD OS alongside the e-Platform 3.0, which is an in-house [operating system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operating_system) for battery electric vehicles that decouples [software](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Software) and hardware. This approach reduces manufacturing and maintenance costs while offering the possibility for other manufacturers using the platform to integrate their own software and hardware.[^cite_note-Rahman-2021-302]

## Manufacturing facilities

See also: [BYD Company § Manufacturing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Company#Manufacturing)

### China

BYD Auto's major passenger car [manufacturing plants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manufacturing_plant) are located in [Xi'an, Shaanxi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xi'an); [Shenzhen, Guangdong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shenzhen,_Guangdong); [Changsha, Hunan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Changsha), [Zhengzhou, Henan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhengzhou,_Henan); [Changzhou, Jiangsu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Changzhou); [Jinan, Shandong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jinan,_Shandong); and [Hefei, Anhui](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hefei,_Anhui). The company also operates R\&D centers in Shenzhen and [Shanghai](27643.md).[^cite_note-wsj1-345]

The Xi'an plant is BYD Auto's first and largest production base, which includes electric assembly, electric motor, and battery production. The first expansion of the plant was completed in September 2014, when the Xi'an BYD No. 2 Plant went operational. BYD added an electric bus plant in the complex in 2017, with an annual capacity of 5,000 units.[^cite_note-346] Following the completion of the third phase development in September 2022, the current annual production capacity of the Xi'an production base is 900,000 vehicles.[^cite_note-topten-347] As of 2024, it is the largest single manufacturing company in the [Shaanxi province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shaanxi).[^cite_note-348] In 2024 alone, the plant produces more than 1 million vehicles.[^cite_note-349]

In late 2012, the Changsha plant went operational with an annual production capacity of 300,000 vehicles. A second phase plant went operational in 2022, making the total production capacity in Changsha reach 300,000 vehicles annually.[^cite_note-topten-347]

BYD Auto started construction of its largest plant in [Hefei, Anhui](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hefei) in July 2021, which started operations in June 2022. It was built in three phases with final annual capacity of 1.32 million vehicles.[^cite_note-topten-347]

In March 2024, the [Ministry of Industry and Information Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Industry_and_Information_Technology) approved BYD's plan to move its passenger vehicle factory in [Pingshan, Shenzhen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pingshan,_Shenzhen) to the Shenzhen-Shantou Special Cooperation Zone in [Shantou, Guangdong](268030.md), 60 km (37 mi) east of Shenzhen.[^cite_note-350]

As of 2024, BYD's annual vehicle production capacity in China has reached 5.82 million vehicles.[^cite_note-\:8-351]

### Outside China



BYD Auto manufacturing plant in [Rayong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rayong), Thailand



BYD Motors Inc. facility in [Lancaster, California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lancaster,_California), in 2016

BYD opened a manufacturing plant for [electric buses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electric_bus) in [Lancaster, California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lancaster,_California), US in May 2013.[^cite_note-356][^cite_note-357] Another plant was inaugurated in [Campinas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Campinas), [Brazil](3383.md) in 2015 for the production of electric buses.[^cite_note-358] BYD also opened a bus plant in 2019 in [Newmarket, Ontario](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newmarket,_Ontario), Canada.[^cite_note-359][^cite_note-360]

BYD has a European electric bus assembly facility in [Komárom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kom%C3%A1rom), [Hungary](13275.md). The plant employed 300 people by the end of 2019, with a production capacity of up to 400 electric buses a year (and 600 chassis) on two shifts.[^cite_note-361]. A significant expansion of the plant was announced in 2025. The capacity will triple and will produce 1,250 electric buses and trucks annually.[^cite_note-362]

In July 2023, BYD announced a US\$620 million investment in [Camaçari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cama%C3%A7ari), Brazil to produce electric cars after acquiring [Ford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ford_Brasil)'s former plant.[^cite_note-363] In July 2024, BYD announced a US\$1 billion investment in Turkey.[^cite_note-364]

In Thailand, BYD operates a manufacturing plant located in the [Eastern Economic Corridor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Economic_Corridor) (EEC) special zone in [Rayong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rayong). Announced in 2023 and with an annual capacity of 150,000 vehicles, construction of the facility began in March of that year. The plant officially opened in July 2024, and was expected to employ 10,000 workers.[^cite_note-\:5-172][^cite_note-\:6-173] On 12 November 2024, BYD Thailand rolled out its 10,000th electric vehicle, just four months after the manufacturing plant went operational.[^cite_note-365]

On 28 April 2025, BYD broke ground on a passenger vehicle manufacturing plant located in Sihanoukville Special Economic Zone, [Preah Sihanouk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Preah_Sihanouk_province), Cambodia.[^cite_note-366]

## Supply chain

BYD Auto is a [vertically integrated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vertical_integration) company that claims to have the highest degree of vertical integration in the world, with over 70% of its vehicle components being supplied independently.[^cite_note-370][^cite_note-371] It produces its own [mouldings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Molding_\(process\)), production lines and equipment, and controls the [supply chain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supply_chain) for most components such as batteries, [electric motors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electric_motor), and electronic controls, which significantly reduced its manufacturing costs and provides a competitive advantage in terms of [profit margins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Profit_margins).[^cite_note-372] BYD established [FinDreams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FinDreams), a brand encompassing five fully owned automotive component manufacturers that also supply parts to other automotive companies in 2020.[^cite_note-373]

A teardown by investment bank [UBS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UBS) of the [BYD Seal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Seal) revealed that 75% of its components were made in-house. Its competition, the [Tesla Model 3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tesla_Model_3) only uses 46% of components that are made in-house in China. UBS concluded that this helped the Seal achieve a gross profit margin of 16%, compared to 14% for the made-in-China Model 3.[^cite_note-Zhou2023-374][^cite_note-375]

### Shipping



BYD Hefei cargo ship near [Livorno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Livorno), Italy

BYD Auto also operates its own shipping services to export its cars.[^cite_note-376] On 9 January 2024, BYD's first [roll-on/roll-off](1457484.md) [cargo ship](786056.md), named "BYD Explorer No.1", was delivered and left the construction base in [Longkou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Longkou), [Shandong province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shandong_province). The 200 m (660 ft)-long ship arrived at [Yantai Port](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yantai_Port) on the same day, before heading to Shenzhen for loading cars for exporting to [Europe](9239.md). According to [China International Marine Containers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_International_Marine_Containers) (CIMC), the ship was built by [Yantai CIMC Raffles Shipyard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yantai_CIMC_Raffles_Shipyard) for the international ship management company [Zodiac Maritime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zodiac_Maritime), and was leased to BYD as the first of its "sea shipping fleet", with a loading capacity of 7,000 vehicles.[^cite_note-377][^cite_note-378][^cite_note-379]

The company's first directly owned ship, the BYD Hefei, was completed in September 2024 and commenced its first voyage to Europe on 7 January 2025. Its third ship is the BYD Changzhou, which was launched in October 2024 and officially went into operation for European deliveries in December 2024. In the spring of 2025, BYD introduced its larger 9,200-vehicle capacity carriers, starting with the BYD Shenzhen, which was officially delivered on 22 April 2025, and kicked off its maiden voyage to Brazil on April 27. Shortly after, the BYD Xi'an, initially launched on 2 April 2025, started its first voyage to Europe on June 21, 2025. The BYD Changsha was officially delivered on 24 June 2025, and made its maiden departure to [Taicang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taicang) on 27 June. The 7,000-vehicle BYD Zhengzhou was named and delivered on 16 July 2025, before setting sail for Southeast Asia on 18 July. The addition of the BYD Jinan, which was delivered on 26 September 2025, completes the initial eight-ship fleet and increased BYD's total maritime export capacity to over one million vehicles annually.[^cite_note-380]

## Other facilities

### Design centre



[BYD E-Seed GT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Han) concept car, 2019

In 2019, BYD launched its global design centre in Shenzhen, China. It is a 12,600-square-meter facility led by European car designers, led by Global Design director [Wolfgang Egger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wolfgang_Egger), BYD Global Exterior Design director JuanMa Lopez and Global Interior Design director Michele Jauch-Paganetti.[^cite_note-389]

The design centre initially housed 200 designers, which has increased to 600 designers by 2023.[^cite_note-390] Its infrastructure includes digital design, physical modeling, and virtual reality evaluation, supported by a 6,000-square-meter clay modeling area equipped with high-precision five-axis processing machinery capable of milling a full-scale [clay model](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clay_model) in five days. Automated clay-cutting tools reduce physical design modification times from several weeks to less than 24 hours, while a micro-spacing 1.2-millimeter LED display system enables full-scale virtual vehicle comparisons and [ergonomic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ergonomic) validations.[^cite_note-391]

In April 2019, the first joint effort from the team, the [E-Seed GT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Han) concept car was showcased at the [Auto Shanghai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auto_Shanghai) in April 2019. The design concept reflected the lines of the traditional Chinese dragon.[^cite_note-392]

### Zhengzhou All-Terrain Circuit

In August 2025, BYD opened an All-Terrain Circuit in [Zhengzhou](288264.md), China, designed specifically for testing and driving new energy vehicles. The facility is open for public, serving as a venue for [track racing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Track_racing), [professional driver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_professional_driver_types) training, and vehicle demonstrations.[^cite_note-393] The circuit features eight specialised zones, including a [Guinness World Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guinness_World_Records)-certified indoor sand dune, a 70-meter wading pool for amphibious maneuvers, and a 1,758-meter paved race track. Handling and stability systems are tested on a low-friction circular track and a kick-plate designed to simulate icy conditions and sudden loss of traction. The facility also includes a 15,300-square-meter dynamic paddock for standardized maneuvers such as the [moose test](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moose_test) and [automated parking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Automatic_parking), a 27-scenario off-road park for assessing all-wheel-drive capabilities, and a dedicated outdoor [camping](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camping) area.[^cite_note-394]

Alongside the opening, BYD and the Federation of Automobile and Motorcycle Sports of China (CAMF) announced the "New Track Scheme," a collaborative program aiming to train 100 professional racers and expand [motorsport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motorsport) engagement.

&#x20;BYD plans to open additional circuits in [Hefei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hefei) and [Shaoxing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shaoxing). The upcoming Shaoxing facility will feature a 2,000-acre off-road testing area situated at an altitude of 500 meters.[^cite_note-395]

## Services

### Vehicle insurance

In May 2023, BYD Auto was approved to acquire a 100% stake in Yi'an P\&C Insurance Co., Ltd., renamed it, and transformed it into its own [insurance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Insurance) company for BYD vehicles.[^cite_note-396][^cite_note-397] In June 2024, BYD Property & Casualty Insurance opened for registrations, and announced the expansion of its car insurance services in 7 major provinces in China.[^cite_note-398] The company has regulatory approval to adjust premium rates independently, addressing high premiums and profitability issues in the new energy vehicle insurance market.[^cite_note-399]

## Partnerships and joint ventures

### &#x20;Toyota joint venture



The [Toyota bZ3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toyota_bZ3), jointly developed between Toyota and BYD

BYD and [Toyota](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toyota) formed a [joint venture](538782.md) called BYD Toyota EV Technology Co., Ltd. (BTET), which was formalised in April 2020 and headquartered in [Shenzhen](83368.md), China.[^cite_note-400] It was established with a focus on the research and development of battery electric vehicles.[^cite_note-401][^cite_note-402] The first product conceived by the joint venture is the [Toyota bZ3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toyota_bZ3), a Chinese market battery electric sedan manufactured by [FAW Toyota](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FAW_Toyota), followed by the [Toyota bZ5](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toyota_bZ5) crossover SUV.

### Hino Motors joint venture

In October 2020, BYD Auto Industry Co., Ltd. signed an agreement with Japanese truck and bus manufacturer [Hino Motors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hino_Motors) to establish a joint venture for commercial battery electric vehicle development. The joint venture planned to introduce vehicles under the Hino brand by 2025.[^cite_note-403]

In February 2023, Hino Motors halted sales of compact electric buses in Japan supplied by BYD due to the presence of [hexavalent chromium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hexavalent_chromium), a [carcinogenic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carcinogenic) chemical banned under Japanese industry guidelines. The affected model is the [Hino Poncho](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hino_Poncho) Z EV.[^cite_note-404] Hino was unable to negotiate with BYD to build the bus without using the chemical, while BYD Japan claims that the buses "meet all required laws and standards".[^cite_note-405]

### KG Mobility

In December 2021, BYD via its battery subsidiary FinDreams Industry Co., Ltd. signed a memorandum of understanding with SsangYong Motor to develop car batteries and produce battery packs for SsangYong's upcoming models, including its first electric vehicle, the U100, planned for mass production from 2023.[^cite_note-406]

In November 2023, the company, by then renamed KG Mobility (KGM) following its acquisition out of receivership, signed an expanded next-generation hybrid system agreement with BYD. The agreement covered battery pack production at a new facility in [Changwon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Changwon), South Korea, and the joint development of full hybrid and plug-in hybrid systems beginning with a [Torres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KGM_Torres)-based hybrid model.[^cite_note-407] The Torres EVX battery electric SUV, introduced in 2023, uses a BYD Blade battery pack.[^cite_note-408]

In January 2026, KGM introduced its first hybrid models, the Torres Hybrid and [Actyon Hybrid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KGM_Actyon), both fitted with a Dual Tech Hybrid system developed in collaboration with BYD and derived from BYD's plug-in hybrid technology, adapted as a conventional full hybrid requiring no external charging. The system pairs a new 1.5 litre turbocharged [Miller cycle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miller_cycle) petrol engine with a P1 and P3 dual motor architecture, and a 1.83 kWh battery within a BYD-derived e-DHT (electrified Dual Hybrid Transmission).[^cite_note-409][^cite_note-410]

### GAC BYD



Guangzhou GZ6850HZEV1 (K7G) bus built by GAC BYD

In August 2014, BYD and [GAC Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GAC_Group) established a joint venture called Guangzhou GAC BYD New Energy Bus Co., Ltd. to produce electric buses in [Guangzhou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guangzhou), the city where GAC is based. BYD holds a 51 percent stake while GAC held 49 percent.[^cite_note-411] The joint venture mainly supplies buses to the local bus operator Guangzhou Bus Group.[^cite_note-412]

### Alexander Dennis

In 2015, BYD partnered with [Alexander Dennis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Dennis) to produce all-electric buses for the British market, including London's first all-electric double-decker.[^cite_note-413] Alexander Dennis built their first-generation [Enviro200EV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Dennis_Enviro200EV) single-deck and [Enviro400EV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Dennis_Enviro400EV) double-deck products on BYD's chassis.[^cite_note-O-2022-61] By 2023, the partnership had produced 1,500 buses.[^cite_note-414]

### Songsan Motor



Songsan SS Dolphin

&#x20;A small Chinese motorcycle manufacturer Songsan Motor contracted BYD to produce [retro-styled cars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Retro-style_automobile), as the company does not have a production license. Its first vehicle, the Songsan SS Dolphin (unrelated to the [BYD Dolphin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Dolphin)) was introduced in 2019 using [Chevrolet Corvette C1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chevrolet_Corvette_\(C1\)) as a design inspiration. It is based on BYD's platform and powered by a plug-in hybrid powertrain. As legally required, the car wears BYD badges alongside Songsan Motor logo.[^cite_note-415] As of 2023, deliveries to customers have not started yet. Songsan sued BYD in September 2023 due to alleged contract disputes and fraudulent environmental certifications, an allegation that was denied by BYD.[^cite_note-416]

## Motorsport

The [Shark 6](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Shark_6) competed and won the Frontera Aventura 2025 Rally in the Dominican Republic in March 2025.[^cite_note-417] In 2026, BYD are officially in talks with the [FIA](248019.md) and opened their interest to start as a new team in [F1](10854.md).[^cite_note-418]

## Lawsuits and controversies

See also: [BYD Company § Lawsuits and disputes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Company#Lawsuits_and_disputes)

### Poor construction labour conditions

#### Brazil

Main article: [BYD Brazil working conditions controversy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Brazil_working_conditions_controversy)

In December 2024, Brazilian authorities announced that they had found 163 Chinese nationals working in "slavery-like conditions" building a BYD plant in [Bahia](199070.md).[^cite_note-419][^cite_note-420] Brazilian labour authorities stated that they were victims of [human trafficking](19333662.md).[^cite_note-421] Brazil's [Ministry of Justice](40581099.md) announced it would revoke residence permits for Chinese workers at the BYD factory if prosecutors confirm irregularities. Jinjiang Group, the contractor for BYD, claimed that Brazilian authorities' characterization of its employees as "enslaved" was "inaccurate" and due to "translation errors". The statement was shared by Li Yunfei, BYD's branding and public relations general manager, who alleged efforts by "foreign forces" to damage the reputation of Chinese brands.[^cite_note-422] Both Jinjiang Group and BYD responded by housing the 163 workers in hotels until a deal to end their contracts is reached. Brazil's [Ministry of Foreign Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Brazil\)) announced that it had stopped issuing temporary work visas for BYD.[^cite_note-423] In May 2025, Brazilian prosecutors filed a lawsuit against BYD.[^cite_note-\:22-18]

#### Hungary

According to the investigation conducted by [China Labor Watch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_Labor_Watch), BYD’s factory development in Szeged, Hungary, also sees poor working conditions. Thousands of workers are required to work every day of the week, often in shifts exceeding 12 hours. Part of the workers' wages have been withheld by the company, deterring the workers from resigning on their own. Many workers went to Hungary without proper visas, making them vulnerable to [exploitation](40067623.md).[^cite_note-msn26-424][^cite_note-DW26-425]

### Marketing and public relations

#### Sponsorship fraud case

In April 2018, British football team [Arsenal F.C.](2174.md) announced BYD as its [sponsorship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sponsor_\(commercial\)) partner. In July 2018, the sponsorship agreement was revealed as fraudulent after BYD claimed the person who signed a sponsorship deal with the club was not authorised to do so. BYD claims that Shanghai police detained a woman who was acting as the marketing manager on suspicion of contract fraud and forging business seals. The individual had signed numerous contracts with advertising agencies, but she has never been an employee of BYD.[^cite_note-426][^cite_note-427]

#### Legal actions against online influencers

In China, BYD has taken legal action towards online [bloggers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bloggers) and social media users. In July 2023, BYD formally sued a [Sina Weibo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sina_Weibo) user, Longzhu-JiChe, alleging that they had been posting false information about BYD. The company demanded a CN¥5 million compensation.[^cite_note-428] In April 2024, BYD filed a lawsuit against a Chinese blogger, Huang, that posted a false claim in social media in January 2023 that BYD instructed its employees to organise consumers to "defend their rights against [Tesla](5533631.md)". The court ruled that Huang must publish a statement of apology and compensate BYD for losses.[^cite_note-429] In May 2024, BYD filed another lawsuit against a Chinese blogger Yao Qiang, demanding that he make a public apology and pay a compensation of CN¥5 million. The blogger claimed the [Fangchengbao Bao 5](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fangchengbao_Bao_5) had a poor fuel consumption of around 18 l/100 km (5.6 km/l; 13 mpg<sub>‑US</sub>). Internet users found traffic violations in the test drive video and reported it to the police. Investigation by BYD revealed that the vehicle was driven abnormally and aggressively.[^cite_note-430]

In mid-2024, following the lead of other automakers and in line with the [Ministry of Public Security](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Public_Security_\(China\))'s "Regulations on the Governance of Online Violence Information," BYD in China introduced a bounty program to report evidence of internet "[black campaigns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_campaign)" conducted by individuals, public relations firms, or rival car companies. Rewards range from 200,000 to 5 million yuan, with confidentiality guaranteed for those who provide information.[^cite_note-431][^cite_note-432] In June 2025, BYD's Legal Department announced that it is taking legal action against 37 online influencer accounts and has placed an additional 126 accounts under internal monitoring for alleged disinformation and damaging content.[^cite_note-433]

By May 2026, based on public court filings and company statements verified by *CarNewsChina*, BYD ranked first among Chinese automakers by total disclosed claim value, having pursued legal action against at least seven blogger accounts with cumulative claims totalling CN¥6,926,300 (approximately US\$1.02 million). The largest individual case involved the blogger account "Long Ge Jiang Dian Che", operated by a repair-shop content creator surnamed Liu, whose posts discussed BYD's Blade Battery and hybrid systems; a second-instance court ruling in May 2026 ordered compensation of CN¥2 million and found that several disputed claims constituted commercial defamation and unfair competition. Other Chinese automakers including [AITO (HIMA)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AITO_\(marque\)), [Nio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nio_Inc.), and [Xpeng](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xpeng) have pursued similar proceedings.[^cite_note-434]

### Price reduction controversies

#### China

BYD has introduced multiple aggressive price reductions in the Chinese market, which have contributed to heightened competition and price war. In early 2023 and early 2024, BYD launched the Champion Edition and Honor Edition variants of its models, respectively. These variants introduced price cuts, intended to increase BYD's market share in China.[^cite_note-\:13-101]

In July 2024, BYD's off-road vehicle brand, Fangchengbao, significantly reduced the price of its Bao 5 model in China by 50,000 yuan to better compete with the popular [Tank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tank_\(marque\)) series by [Great Wall Motor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Wall_Motor).[^cite_note-435] The price reduction had sparked controversy among previous buyers. Protests occurred with banners criticizing Fangchengbao's sudden price cut, as some customers expressed frustration after waiting months for delivery only to see the price drop soon after.[^cite_note-436][^cite_note-437]

In early 2025, BYD avoided price cuts in favour of offering [self-driving](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Self-driving_car) systems as standard.[^cite_note-\:13-101] However, in May 2025, BYD announced substantial price reductions across 22 models, with discounts reaching up to 34%, due to increasing [inventory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inventory) levels. The BYD Seagull experienced a 20% reduction to 55,800 yuan, while the BYD Han DM-p was discounted by 34% to 102,800 yuan. The move further intensified the ongoing price war, and triggered broad concern within the industry. Great Wall Motor chairman [Wei Jianjun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wei_Jianjun) publicly compared the situation to the pre-collapse state of the [Evergrande](16207770.md) real estate group.[^cite_note-438][^cite_note-439][^cite_note-440] At the same time, BYD, along with [Dongfeng Motor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dongfeng_Motor_Corporation) and other Chinese automakers were summoned by [China's Ministry of Commerce](638506.md) to attend a meeting addressing the rising number of "zero-mileage" [used car](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Used_car) sales, shortly after Great Wall Motor chairman, Wei Jianjun, also highlighted an ongoing trend in which brand new vehicles were registered and sold on used car platforms. This sales practice has been linked to strategies used by automakers and dealers to meet sales targets. As a result of the news, shares of BYD declined by 3.1%.[^cite_note-441][^cite_note-\:14-19]

#### Thailand

In June 2024, BYD came under investigation by Thai authorities, including the Consumer Protection Commission (CPC) and the Office of the Consumer Protection Board (OCPB), regarding its practices in offering price cuts in the Thai market. The investigation was prompted by a complaint from a customer, stating that a salesperson on 19 June had convinced them to purchase a BYD Dolphin by claiming that prices would increase after the discount period ended. Instead, BYD reduced the car's price by 140,000–160,000 baht on 1 July, causing the customer to experience "psychological distress".[^cite_note-442][^cite_note-443] On 5 July, the issue was discussed by Thailand Prime Minister [Srettha Thavisin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Srettha_Thavisin) and BYD CEO Wang Chuanfu shortly after they both opened the BYD Thailand manufacturing plant. Wang promised to resolve the issue.[^cite_note-444]

### Manufacturing plant pollution



Protests at the BYD Auto Industry Changsha manufacturing plant

In May 2022, [Changsha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Changsha) city authorities opened an investigation into a manufacturing plant owned by BYD Auto in the city following complaints from nearby residents. The allegations surfaced in April 2022 on a website belonging to state newspaper *[People's Daily](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Daily)*, where people living near the factory left messages claiming the plant's pollution had a strong odour, which gave residents irritated throats and [nosebleeds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nosebleeds) to children. Changsha residents purchased testing equipments and discovered that [total volatile organic compound](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TVOC) levels have exceeded safety standards.[^cite_note-Asia_Financial-2022-445] Residents protested at the Changsha plant's gate, urging the company to solve the problem. BYD denied the allegations, stating that all emissions from the plant were compliant with China's regulatory rules and standards, and threatened legal actions against people who spread rumours that emissions from the factory had caused nosebleeds.[^cite_note-Asia_Financial-2022-445][^cite_note-446][^cite_note-447] Production in the plant was partially halted during the investigation.[^cite_note-\:15-20]

### Emissions non-compliance allegations

In May 2023, rival [Great Wall Motor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Wall_Motor) (GWM) publicly reported through its social media that two BYD top-selling plug-in hybrid models did not meet [emission standards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vehicle_emission_standard). In a social media post, GWM accused BYD of using non-pressurised [fuel tanks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fuel_tanks) in its [Qin Plus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Qin_Plus) and [Song Plus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BYD_Song_Plus), enabling the liquid inside to evaporate more rapidly than it would in pressurised tanks, and announced that it had submitted evidence documents to Chinese authorities.[^cite_note-448] BYD denied GWM's claim, saying the testing conducted by GWM was invalid as it had run its own tests by using a different testing method than required by authorities, and lacked third-party inspection.[^cite_note-449][^cite_note-450]

### Dealership fires

In May 2024, fire broke out in a BYD dealership in [Fuzhou, Fujian.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fuzhou) The Chinese language site of American news outlet [NTDTV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Tang_Dynasty_Television) reported that it is the tenth BYD dealership fire since 2021. One BYD dealership fire case was reported in 2021, followed by four cases in 2022 and another four in 2023. Two factory fire cases were recorded in 2022 and 2023. BYD acknowledged the Fuzhou incident, but denied that the fire came from any of its vehicles and detected no abnormalities in the vehicle batteries during its inspection.[^cite_note-451][^cite_note-452]

### Alliance for American Manufacturing lawsuit

In November 2020, BYD in the U.S. filed a lawsuit against the [Alliance for American Manufacturing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alliance_for_American_Manufacturing) (AAM), a non-profit [lobbying group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lobbying_group) that advocated for a ban on using the federal tax budget to purchase rail cars or buses from foreign state-owned or controlled companies such like BYD, which has an electric bus factory in California, U.S. The rule was signed into law as the Transit Infrastructure Vehicle Security Act (TIVSA). BYD alleges that AAM has engaged in a "malicious, fraudulent, outrageous, and reckless campaign" that damages BYD's reputation.[^cite_note-453] The [Supreme Court of the United States](31737.md) rejected BYD's suit in August 2022.[^cite_note-454]

### Cybersecurity concerns

In July 2025, Israel's [Ministry of Defense](3226446.md) prohibited the use of certain BYD electric cars due to claimed cybersecurity concerns of unauthorized transmission of driver data to China.[^cite_note-455]

In August 2025, Taiwan's [Ministry of Economic Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Economic_Affairs_\(Taiwan\)) restricted imports of BYD cars assembled in Thailand from entering the Taiwanese market due to considerations of national security, vehicle safety, information security and industrial development.[^cite_note-456]

### Quality concerns of buses

#### Quality issues in the United States

In December 2018, the [Albuquerque, New Mexico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albuquerque,_New_Mexico) council in the US sued BYD over the alleged poor welding, poor battery range, and malfunctioning doors and brakes of its buses.[^cite_note-457] BYD denied the allegations, and the lawsuit was settled without money changing hands.[^cite_note-458]

A 2018 investigation by *[The Los Angeles Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Los_Angeles_Times)* found quality and reliability issues with the BYD buses used by public transit agencies in [California](5407.md), such as frequent stalls and unpredictable driving ranges below advertised figures.[^cite_note-John2018-459]

#### Toxic chemical use in Japan

In February 2023, BYD confirmed the presence of a toxic chemical, [hexavalent chromium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hexavalent_chromium), in its electric buses in Japan. According to BYD, the contaminated vehicles will be "detoxified before being scrapped, leaving no impact on the environment."[^cite_note-460]

#### Fraud and quality issues in the Netherlands



A BYD K9UB of Keolis in [Barneveld](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barneveld_\(town\))

In December 2019, [Keolis Nederland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keolis_Nederland) ordered over 250 buses from BYD for the [IJssel-Vecht](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IJssel-Vecht?action=edit\&redlink=1) region in Eastern Netherlands, which was the largest order for electric buses in Europe at the time.[^cite_note-461][^cite_note-462] Keolis was awarded a 10-year contract to run public transportation in the region starting in December 2020. In July 2020, it was revealed that Keolis did not provide all essential information in the bid to the provinces and tenderers. Keolis had made secret arrangements with BYD that was not disclosed during the bid, meaning that the company had essentially committed fraud to win the tender.[^cite_note-463] Keolis was allowed to start operating in the area as part of an emergency [concession](3261664.md) as finding a new operator to takeover in such short notice would put the entire bus network in danger. The new contract running for 13 years from 2022 onward was awarded to [EBS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egged_\(company\)) instead, which would take over 234 of the 259 BYD buses of Keolis.[^cite_note-Vijf-464][^cite_note-465]

BYD delivered the buses at the end of 2020. Immediately, problems arose with the quality and reliability of the buses, generally related with the electronics and the software. The most significant reported issue was the battery, which would be shown as full on the dashboard, only to suddenly empty out, leading to buses being stranded mid-route.[^cite_note-Vijf-464] Another prominent issue was the bus veering to the right while driving, causing the drivers to constantly steer to the left. Additionally, the driver's seat was not centered around the steering wheel and was too small in most cases. Because of the steering and seat issues, drivers have complained of experiencing physical pain during and after driving. While EBS was aware of the quality issues surrounding the BYD buses, these turned out to be "more numerous and persistent" than initially expected.[^cite_note-466] The three companies involved have reached an agreement regarding the cost of repairs, though the contents of these have not been made public.[^cite_note-467]

### Electric bus subsidy fraud

In 2016, a [suicide note](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_note) by a dealership owner in [Nanjing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nanjing) led to an investigation into government subsidy [fraud](58790.md). The note claimed that BYD had received subsidies for 600 electric buses it had never produced, with only a few dozen delivered to the city government. BYD reportedly forced dealerships to increase electric vehicle inventories, selling to people outside of Nanjing and helping them obtain temporary residence documents. The dealerships ended up paying subsidies on 80 percent of vehicles sold.[^cite_note-ChinaFile-2016-468] Following the case, the Chinese Finance Ministry fined five manufacturers (not including BYD) for fraudulently obtaining over CN¥1 billion worth of subsidies. The companies were required to return the subsidies and pay penalties worth an additional 50% of the subsidies received.[^cite_note-469]

BYD car sales in 2009 were 448,400 vehicles. Its F3 was the best-selling sedan in China with sales of over 291,000 units.[^cite_note-Anderson-2012-51][^cite_note-473] In 2010, BYD sold 519,800 vehicles, representing 2.9% of the market in China, its sixth largest manufacturer.[^cite_note-wsj1-345][^cite_note-474] In 2011, the BYD sales rank was outside the top ten.[^cite_note-475] In 2012, the company became the ninth largest car manufacturer in China, producing over 600,000 vehicles.[^cite_note-476] In 2013, BYD sold 506,189 passenger cars in China, ranking tenth.[^cite_note-477]

### 2021–present: Rapid sales increase

Since 2021, BYD's sales have grown significantly, driven in part by the rapid increase in new energy vehicle sales as a share of total passenger vehicle sales in China during 2021 and 2022.[^cite_note-O-2023-78] While the new energy vehicle market share were stagnant between 4–5% since 2018, in 2021 and 2022, new energy vehicles accounted for 15.4 and 27.5% all passenger vehicle sales respectively. Within BYD's total vehicle sales, new energy vehicles also accounted for an increasing percentage from 52.4% in 2018 into reaching 99.2% in the first half of 2022, shortly before the company ended production of pure internal combustion engine vehicles.[^cite_note-478]

In the first half of 2022, BYD becomes the top plug-in electric vehicle seller in the world after surpassing [Tesla](5533631.md) by selling 641,000 vehicles.[^cite_note-South_China_Morning_Post-2022-479] In September 2022, BYD became the first carmaker in China to build one million new energy vehicles in a single year.[^cite_note-\:11-87]

In late 2022 and early 2023, BYD plug-in hybrid vehicles surpassed the sales of its battery electric vehicles, which was partly attributed to the positive reception of BYD's DM-i system that replaced the older DM system in January 2021.[^cite_note-480]

The company surpassed Tesla as the world's top battery electric vehicle manufacturer in the fourth quarter of 2023 by selling 526,409 battery electric cars, while Tesla delivered 484,507 vehicles.[^cite_note-Kane-2024-481][^cite_note-482] Meanwhile, throughout 2023, BYD remained the world's largest plug-in hybrid electric vehicle manufacturer and the second-largest battery electric vehicle manufacturer (after Tesla), with global market shares of 21.4% and 15%, respectively.[^cite_note-483][^cite_note-484] In that period, 52.5% of BYD's sales volume were contributed by battery electric vehicles (including commercial vehicles).[^cite_note-CNC2023-4]

BYD became the top-selling car brand in China ahead of [Volkswagen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volkswagen) in 2023, marking the first time another company has sold more cars than Volkswagen in the country.[^cite_note-BloombergVW-13] In early 2024, BYD sales in China were briefly surpassed by Volkswagen, ending its 11-month streak as the top-selling brand in China.[^cite_note-485] This was attributed to the 2024 Chinese New Year holiday and reduced demand.[^cite_note-486] BYD regained the top position by the second quarter of 2024.[^cite_note-487] In that quarter, BYD global sales has surpassed [Honda](13729.md) and [Nissan](21240.md), making it the seventh-largest automaker in the world.[^cite_note-488]

BYD ended 2024 achieving 4,272,145 sales, a 41.3% increase compared to 2023.[^cite_note-489][^cite_note-490] In the Chinese domestic market, with 3,718,281 retail sales, BYD is the market leader with a 16.2% share (all vehicles) and 34.1% share in the new energy vehicle category. Plug-in hybrid vehicles contributed about 60% of its wholesale sales in that year.[^cite_note-491]

[JPMorgan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/JPMorgan) expects BYD to sell 6.5 million vehicles in 2026.[^cite_note-\:10-139]
